## [R07.19] - 2026-10-02 1:00:13 PM

**Primary User chat prompt + host-environment probe in the system prompt.** The chat REPL now asks who is chatting and renders their name instead of the hardcoded `You:`. At Agent construction, a light stdlib-only probe detects the OS family + version and appends a `# Host Environment` section to the system prompt, so the model knows which argument syntax to pass to the shell tool (cmd.exe on Windows vs `/bin/sh` POSIX on Linux/macOS). Follow-up commits in the same release also make the `agentkthx models` Name column dynamic (ROB-37) so long model names can no longer break the table grid, rename the Primary User prompt examples to `VTSTech:`, render real example arguments in the Tool Reference table instead of `"..."` placeholders, rename the bundled souls `nova-*` → `kthx-*` with an accuracy review of the renamed `kthx-helper`, add `/souls` + `/soul` chat commands with a mid-session soul switch, complete the `agentkthx config` environment-variable reference, sort every `-h` output alphabetically, ship an interactive arrow-key model picker (an in-chat `/models` switcher plus a startup picker when `--model` is omitted), add the `agentkthx souls` listing subcommand, ship a capabilities-based Ollama tool-support probe (`scripts/probe_ollama_tools.py`) that replaces sampled probing with the server's own `/api/tags` capabilities verdict, wire that same capabilities signal into `OllamaBackend.test_tool_support` so `agentkthx models --tool-support` and chat-time auto-detection get authoritative verdicts without loading models (sampled probe retained only for legacy servers), collapse the models-table dual `openre`/`openai` tool-support columns into ONE `tools` column fed by a single API-mode-independent capability check — with every would-be none verdict falling back to ReAct so no model is ever classified none (follow-up #10) — and add a `think` column exposing per-model thinking/reasoning support for free off the same capabilities declaration (follow-up #11). The ZAI tool-support probe is then hardened after a live misclassification: the free tier rate-limits rapid sequential probes (HTTP 429 code 1302) and the old probe cached REACT for ANY HTTP error, so one rate-limited run mislabeled the native glm-4.7-flash as react — transient failures now retry with backoff and return UNTESTED without touching the cache, and the probe disables thinking + raises max_tokens so a reasoning model's budget can never truncate the tool call away (follow-up #12).

### Added — Primary User in chat

- **Interactive naming prompt at session start** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/chat.py`): before the REPL loop takes over, chat resolves the Primary User via `_resolve_primary_user()` — resolution order: `--user` CLI flag → `AGENTKTHX_USER` env var → interactive prompt (`Primary User [<os-login>]: `, Enter accepts the default, TTY-only so piped stdin / ACP / scripting never block) → `getpass.getuser()` → `"You"`. Ctrl+C/Ctrl+D at the naming prompt falls through to the default instead of killing the session.
- **Named REPL prompt**: the `You:` prompt is replaced by `<PrimaryUser>:` on every turn, in both branches — readline (`\001\033[33m\002<name>:\001\033[0m\002 ` with zero-width markers) and no-readline fallback (`\033[33m<name>:\033[0m `, proper CSI form — ROB-34 closed, see below).
- **Input sanitization** (`_sanitize_primary_user`): the name is interpolated into an `input()` prompt on every turn, so control characters / ANSI escapes are stripped and length is capped at 32 chars before it ever reaches the terminal.
- **`--user` / `-u` CLI flag** (`agentkthx/cli/parser.py`, chat subcommand only): skips the startup naming prompt. `AGENTKTHX_USER` env var does the same for scripted launches. The flag is deliberately NOT on `agent`/`run`/`test` (non-interactive surfaces) — pinned by test.

### Added — Host Environment probe in the system prompt

- **New `agentkthx/core/environment.py`**: light, stdlib-only, zero-subprocess probe — `platform`/`sys` reads plus one `/etc/os-release` parse (`platform.freedesktop_os_release`). Total cost is microseconds, paid ONCE per `Agent()` construction, never per turn.
- **Per-family detection**: Windows → `Windows 11 (NT 10.0 build 22631)` / `Windows 10 (NT 10.0 build 19045)` (Win11 = build ≥ 22000; other NT majors render as `Windows (NT x.y)`); Linux → distro `PRETTY_NAME` + kernel (`Ubuntu 24.04.1 LTS, kernel 6.8.0-45-generic`, falls back to kernel-only when os-release is missing/malformed, PRETTY_NAME capped at 80 chars); macOS → `macOS 15.2, Darwin 24.2.0`; anything else → `Unknown (<system> <release>)`. Architecture (`platform.machine()`) included in every line.
- **Shell-syntax guidance** — the point of the section. The `shell` tool runs `subprocess.run(shell=True)`, which means **cmd.exe** on Windows but **/bin/sh** on POSIX. The `# Host Environment` section states this explicitly per family: Windows gets the cmd.exe note + `powershell -Command "..."` escape hatch; Linux gets POSIX/GNU-coreutils; macOS gets the BSD-userland warning (`sed -i ''` suffix, readlink flags). Small local models routinely emit `ls` on Windows or `Get-ChildItem` through cmd.exe — this closes that gap at the prompt level.
- **Wired into ALL prompt paths** (`agentkthx/core/agent_setup.py`): the section is appended after soul/default/custom-prompt assembly and after `skills_prompt`, so every session carries it regardless of prompt origin. Stored with the system message in memory (persistent sessions included).
- **Fail-safe by contract**: `build_environment_section()` returns `""` on ANY probe failure — a broken os-release must never break Agent construction (same best-effort contract as the soul loader).
- **`AGENTKTHX_NO_ENV_PROBE=1` opt-out**: skips the probe entirely (same truthiness convention as `AGENTKTHX_DEBUG`, same pattern as `AGENTKTHX_NO_UPDATE_CHECK`).
- **BitNet compact mode**: BitNet's lean prompt must stay under ~500 chars (reserved-token-ID crash threshold), so BitNet sessions get a single plain `Environment: Windows 11 (NT 10.0 build 22631), AMD64; shell tool runs cmd.exe syntax.` line instead of the markdown section.

### Fixed — ROB-34 (audit register)

- **ROB-34 CLOSED**: the finding described the no-readline fallback prompt as a bare-ESC string (`"\033You:\033 "` — `ESC Y` consumed as a 2-byte VT escape, prompt rendering as `ou:`). Byte-level verification of the R07.18 tree showed the code already carried the proper CSI form (`"\033[33mYou:\033[0m "`), so the defect was not present as filed; R07.19 then rewrote both prompt branches entirely (named prompt), and source-level pins in the new test file assert the bare-ESC shape can never return. Full rationale in `audit/deltas.md` (archived section) and the R07.19 delta note in `audit/audit.md`.

### Fixed — ROB-37 (audit register, follow-up commit)

- **ROB-37 CLOSED** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/models.py`): R07.18 fixed the local Name column at 48 chars (cloud: 50) while keeping the no-truncation policy — the user needs the full name to copy into `-m`, and `pad_colored` pads short names but cannot absorb long ones. Real-world trigger (user report on the R07.19 pre-release build): `krith/meta-llama-3.2-1b-instruct-uncensored:IQ4_XS` (50 chars, 2 over the floor) pushed its row's Size/Quant/Context columns right and broke the grid. `NAME_W` is now measured from the longest model name in the already-loaded (and free-filtered) `models` list — `max(48 local / 50 cloud, longest_name)` — computed BEFORE the header/separator render, so header, separator and every data row share one width. Both layout branches grow with the measured width (local separator `76 + NAME_W`, cloud `43 + NAME_W`); the floors are unchanged, so short listings render byte-identical to R07.18. Cloud providers get the same dynamic behavior.
- Full rationale in `audit/deltas.md` (archived ROB-37 section) and the R07.19 delta notes in `audit/audit.md`.

### Fixed — Tool Reference real example arguments (follow-up commit #2)

- The system prompt's Tool Reference table rendered every string parameter as the generic `"..."` placeholder — the user reported the shell row (`{"command": "...", "timeout": 10}`) reads as if the prompt itself were truncated, and a placeholder gives small local models nothing to copy (they render the Arguments column verbatim — the same mechanism that made the old `0` timeout example dangerous). String params now render real values: curated per param name (`_PARAM_STRING_EXAMPLES` in `agentkthx/soul/loader.py` — shell `command` → `echo Hello, World!`, 16 names covering the builtin registry), else the param's own non-empty string default (todo `priority` → `medium`), else `...`. Enum params show their first always-valid value, booleans render `true`, objects render `{}`.
- The "When to use" cell cap moved 40 → 60 chars with word-boundary cutting — `Execute shell commands (with security restrictions)` now fits in full instead of `(with security...`.

### Changed — nova-* souls renamed to kthx-* + kthx-helper accuracy review (follow-up commit #3)

- **The three bundled souls rename to match the project branding** — `nova-helper` → `kthx-helper`, `nova-skills` → `kthx-skills`, `nova-trading` → `kthx-trading` (directories via `git mv`, `soul.json` `name`, every AGENTS.md self-reference, and the trading soul's `Nova Trading Analyst` display branding → `Kthx Trading Analyst` in `displayName`/`SOUL.md`/`IDENTITY.md`). The souls were the last user-visible surfaces still carrying the pre-R06.0 AgentNova naming. The `--soul` flag now takes the new names; any script still passing `--soul nova-helper` must switch to `--soul kthx-helper`.
- **All live references adjusted** (`agentkthx/core/agent_setup.py` constructor default + docstring + comment, a `soul/loader.py` comment, the ARCH-04 regression pins in `tests/test_r07_13_arch_closures.py`, `docs/TESTS.md` (25 sites), `docs/ARCH.md` (directory tree rows, the `load_soul` example, and the stale `nova-trading` tree comment corrected from "crypto-signals demo" to "TSX/TSX-V paper trading"), `audit/brief.md`, and the MAINT-24 finding's soul example in `audit/audit.md`). Historical changelog sections keep their period-correct `nova-*` mentions — they record what the files were called at the time.
- **kthx-helper accuracy review** (user request — the soul hadn't been revisited in a long time), verified against the current tree: all 16 `allowedTools` names exist in the 17-tool builtin registry (`web_search` deliberately excluded — AGENTS.md documents its absence as an escalation trigger); the static Tool Reference table's argument names all match the registry (`expression`/`command`/`file_path`/`content`/`path`/`timezone`/`code`); every `{{...}}` placeholder in SOUL.md is loader-substituted (all 5 supported); AGENTS.md's orchestrator claims hold (`AgentCard.tools`/`priority`/`fallback` fields, the `concat`/`first`/`vote`/`best` merge strategies, pipeline forwarding the Final Answer only).
- **One defect fixed in SOUL.md**: the Common Mistakes hallucination example called `` `17 - 9` `` WRONG ("where did 17 and 9 come from?") while the Time Calculation section teaches `17 - 9` as the CORRECT 9 AM→5 PM expression (5 PM → 17) — contradictory guidance for the small models this soul targets. The example now hallucinates `` `15 - 4` `` against the running 24/8/6 apples example.

### Added — /souls + /soul chat commands + config env-var reference (follow-up commit #5)

- **`/souls` chat command** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/chat.py`): lists every bundled Soul Spec package in the chat REPL — `✓` marks the active soul, `○` the available ones — with bare name, version and a 60-char description, mirroring the `/skills` layout. Backed by the new `SoulLoader.list_souls()` discovery API (`agentkthx/soul/loader.py`): scans the bundled `agentkthx/souls/` root (importlib.resources first, filesystem fallback — same resolution order as `load()`), loads each `<dir>/soul.json` at Level 1 (manifest only — display metadata without the persona-file I/O), skips unloadable entries best-effort, returns manifests sorted by directory name. Mirrors `SkillLoader.list_skills()`.
- **`/soul` chat command**: no argument shows the active soul (display name + bare name + version, disclosure level, description, allowed tools) or explains that the default prompt is in use; `/soul <name>` switches the persona mid-session via the new `AgentSetupMixin.switch_soul()` (`agentkthx/core/agent_setup.py`), which mirrors the startup `--soul` path exactly — load the manifest at the session's `--soul-level` (overridable), re-apply the `allowedTools` filter to the CURRENT tool registry (startup contract: tools can be filtered out, never added — mid-session `/tool` additions are respected as the base), rebuild the system prompt (tool-aware soul prompt → accumulated `--skills`/`/skill` text → `# Host Environment` section), refresh the ToolParser name list, and swap the system message in memory (`Memory.add("system", ...)` drops the previous one). Conversation history is untouched. Unknown souls get a fuzzy-match suggestion (`did you mean 'kthx-trading'?`) plus the available list, like `/skill`.
- **Skill accumulation across switches**: the `/skill` command now also appends each loaded skill block to the new `agent._skills_prompt` accumulator (initialized from the startup `--skills` text in `agent_setup`), so a soul switch rebuilds the prompt with EVERY loaded skill — startup and mid-session — instead of silently dropping the mid-session ones.
- **`agentkthx config` env-var reference completeness**: the reference at the bottom of the default view was missing every variable added after its last revision — the entire **Mistral** (8 vars), **OrcaRouter** (7 vars) and **Pollinations** (8 vars) plugin surfaces, the R07.19 core vars (`AGENTKTHX_USER`, `AGENTKTHX_NO_ENV_PROBE`, `AGENTKTHX_NO_UPDATE_CHECK`), resilience/loop knobs (`AGENTKTHX_MAX_API_RETRIES`, `AGENTKTHX_PARALLEL_TOOLS`), integration vars (`AGENTKTHX_ACP`, `AGENTKTHX_ACP_URL`, `AGENTKTHX_PLUGIN_PATH`, `AGENTKTHX_USER_AGENT`), `OPENAI_MAX_429_RETRIES`, `OLLAMA_MODELS`, `HF_BASE_URL_LEGACY`, and a new **Display / platform** group (`NO_COLOR`, `CLICOLOR`, `CLICOLOR_FORCE`, `AGENTKTHX_GLYPHS`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, `XDG_STATE_HOME`). The reference now lists 96 variables with zero stale entries. `--urls` gains the Mistral/OrcaRouter/Pollinations backend URLs (9 → 12) and `--full` gains the three plugin sections (masked keys, same layout as the existing OpenRouter/ZAI/Gemini/HF/OpenAI sections); the default summary's Backend URLs list also gains the three backends so the ` * ` active-backend marker works for them.

### Changed — every `-h` output sorted alphabetically (follow-up commit #6)

- **All `-h` outputs now render options A→Z** (user report: `agentkthx chat -h` listed flags in registration order — the `add_agent_args()` build order — which made flags hard to find once the shared set grew past 30 entries; the same applied to every other subcommand's help). A new `SortedHelpFormatter` (`agentkthx/cli/parser.py`) sorts both the "options:" listing AND the `usage:` line by each flag's primary long name: `-m MODEL, --model MODEL` sorts under "model", `-h, --help` under "help", `-u USER, --user USER` under "user" (last in `chat -h`), `--temp/--temperature` under "temp", and the bare `--` llama-server passthrough in `turbo start -h` sorts first. Positional arguments keep their registration order everywhere (`run prompt`, `soul path`, `turbo start model`) — their order is semantic, so they are exempt from the sort.
- **The formatter is applied recursively and to plugin commands**: `apply_sorted_help()` sets `formatter_class` on the root parser and every subparser below it (argparse reads `formatter_class` at help-render time, so post-construction assignment covers fully built parsers), runs at the end of `create_parser()` and again in `main()` after plugin CLI subparsers are registered (idempotent). It also re-sorts each `_SubParsersAction` registry IN PLACE — `choices` is the same dict as `_name_parser_map`, so rebinding would desync parse lookup from the rendered metavar, and the `_choices_actions` help listing is sorted alongside — which means late-registered plugin commands (e.g. the shipped `plugin-test`) land in alphabetical position instead of appending after `version`.
- **Subcommand registration order alphabetized**: `plugins` now registers before `run`, `tools` before `turbo`, and `turbo status` before `turbo stop`, so the root `-h` metavar reads `{agent,chat,config,modelfile,models,plugins,run,sessions,skills,soul,test,tools,turbo,update,version}` and the turbo subcommand list reads `list, start, status, stop`. Dispatch is name-keyed, so this is display-only. (Follow-up #7 adds `souls` after `soul`, keeping the metavar alphabetical.)

### Added — arrow-key model picker: interactive `/models` switch, optional `--model` at chat startup, `agentkthx souls` (follow-up commit #7)

- **New `agentkthx/cli/picker.py` — `ArrowMenu`, a pure-stdlib arrow-key single-select menu.** Zero third-party imports (runtime zero-dep contract, pinned by an AST test): rendering is separated from terminal I/O so the whole core (`move_up/down/home/end/pgup/pgdn`, sliding `visible`-tall viewport, `render()` frame) is unit-testable without a terminal. POSIX reads keys via `termios`+`tty` in **cbreak** mode (ISIG stays on, so Ctrl+C raises `KeyboardInterrupt` inside the loop and maps to *cancel* — it can never kill the host chat session; the terminal state is restored in a `finally`); Windows uses `msvcrt.getwch()` with the `\xe0`-prefix scan-code table; a lone Esc is told apart from an escape sequence by a `select()` peek after the ESC byte. Navigation: ↑/↓ or j/k, g/G or Home/End, PgUp/PgDn, Enter selects, q/Esc/Ctrl+C/Ctrl+D cancels. The frame is redrawn in place with cursor-up + clear-line (no scrollback spam) and wiped on exit — the caller prints a one-line outcome instead; the viewport is clamped to the terminal height so the frame always fits the chat scroll region. Non-TTY stdin (piped scripts, tests, ACP) falls back to a numbered list read from stdin, so the component degrades gracefully everywhere instead of hanging on raw-mode reads.
- **`/models` in chat is now an interactive switcher** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/chat.py`): on a real terminal the command opens the arrow-key menu over the same (filtered) model list — `/models free` / `/models chat` filters still pre-filter the items — with the current model marked `✓` and pre-selected. Enter switches via the SAME `apply_model_switch()` path as `/model <name>` (ROB-14: num_ctx/num_predict/family config re-derive, explicitly pinned values survive), so picking from the menu is byte-equivalent to typing the name; re-selecting the current model is a no-op (`Already on ...`), Esc/q cancels (`Cancelled — model unchanged.`). Piped stdin keeps the plain listing (scriptable surfaces unchanged). The outcome printing moved into a shared `_report_model_switch()` helper used by BOTH `/model <name>` and the picker.
- **`-m/--model` is now optional on `agentkthx chat`**: when omitted, no `AGENTKTHX_MODEL` override is set, no ACP session is driving the process and stdin is a TTY, the arrow-key picker runs BEFORE the agent is built (`_startup_model_pick()` — cloud backends probed in OPENAI mode, local in OPENRE, mirroring `agentkthx models`), so the session starts on the model you picked; `config.default_model` is pre-marked `✓`. Everything else keeps the classic resolution inside `_build_agent` (bitnet server discovery → `config.default_model`): listing failures, empty backends and cancellations all degrade to the default model with a yellow notice instead of blocking the session. Non-interactive surfaces (`run`, `agent`, `test`) are unchanged by design — a TUI picker in a scripted `agentkthx run` would break automation.
- **New `agentkthx souls` subcommand** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/souls.py`, registered between `soul` and `test`): the CLI counterpart of the chat `/souls` command and sibling of `agentkthx skills`. Bare `agentkthx souls` lists every bundled Soul Spec package (`✓` marks the constructor-default soul — introspected from `AgentSetupMixin.__init__`'s `soul` parameter default, no literal to drift) with name, version and a 60-char description, plus usage hints (`--soul <name>`, mid-session `/soul <name>`, `agentkthx souls <name>`). `agentkthx souls <name>` prints a manifest-level detail view for ONE bundled soul resolved BY NAME (display name, version/spec, author, license, category, tags, allowed tools with count, recommended skills, compatible models) — the missing bridge between the bare-name `--soul` flag and the by-path `agentkthx soul <path>` deep inspector; unknown names get a fuzzy-match suggestion (`did you mean 'kthx-trading'?`) plus the available list.

### Added — capabilities-based Ollama tool-support probe (follow-up commit #8)

- **New `scripts/probe_ollama_tools.py`** (user-driven pivot): after walking through the sampled `test_tool_support` logic, the user suspected capable models were being misclassified as `react` (the bare probe sends no system prompt, caps at 100 tokens, and classifies generic HTTP errors as react) and asked for a live-probe script that uses the real system prompt — then simplified the whole approach one step further by pointing out that modern Ollama already declares per-model tool support in `GET /api/tags`' `capabilities` array. The server derives that field from the model's chat template / GGUF metadata — the exact same source its runner consults at request time — so `"tools" in capabilities` IS the authoritative verdict, at O(1) cost, zero model loading, zero inference, zero false negatives from sampling. The script lists every model with family / quant / size / parameter count / verdict (`✓ native` | `✗ none`) / the raw capabilities list, cross-references the local `tool_support.json` cache written by `agentkthx models --tool-support` and marks cached verdicts that disagree with the declared capability with `!` (surfacing the old probe's false negatives), warns when the server predates the capabilities field, and takes `--json` (JSONL), `--no-cache`, a positional base URL and `$OLLAMA_BASE_URL`. No AgentKthx imports — pure stdlib + one GET.
- **Live-verified against a Cloudflare-tunneled Ollama host**: 10 models classified in under a second — 7 native (`functiongemma:270m`, `granite4:350m`, `nchapman/dolphin3.0-qwen2.5:0.5b`, `qwen2.5:0.5b`, `qwen2.5-coder:0.5b/1.5b`, `krith/meta-llama-3.2-1b-instruct-uncensored:IQ4_XS`) / 3 none (`gemma3:270m`, `qwen2:0.5b`, `nchapman/dolphin3.0-llama3:1b`), matching the declared capabilities exactly. Notable: `functiongemma:270m` is tool-capable while sibling `gemma3:270m` is not — a distinction the family-based `model_family_config` cannot express but the capabilities field does.

### Changed — `--tool-support` now reads the server's capabilities verdict (follow-up commit #9)

- **`OllamaBackend.test_tool_support` is capabilities-first** (`agentkthx/backends/ollama.py`, user-driven pivot from follow-up #8): `agentkthx models --tool-support` no longer loads each model to run the 100-token "What's the weather in Tokyo?" sample — for any modern Ollama the verdict comes straight from `GET /api/tags`' per-model `capabilities` field (`"tools"` → NATIVE, absent → NONE), which the server derives from the model's chat template / GGUF metadata: the same source its runner consults at request time. The declaration is authoritative where the sample was statistical — the old probe sent no system prompt (capable-but-chatty models answered in plain text and were misclassified as react), capped at 100 tokens (thinking models burned the budget before emitting a call), and classified generic HTTP errors as react. One GET serves the whole run: a new memoized `_capabilities_map()` (per-instance, `model_capabilities()` accessor) fetches `/api/tags` once and answers every model in both API modes; the verdict is still written to the persistent tool_support cache so cross-process consumers (`agent_factory` reads) see it without a fetch. API-mode-independent by nature — the runner template governs openre and openai alike, so the two columns now legitimately agree.
- **The sampled probe survives as a legacy fallback** for servers that predate the capabilities field and for models absent from the listing — no declaration means no fabricated verdict. Those fall through to the old cache-then-sample path unchanged; a capabilities-bearing response that omits the field for one model is likewise treated as no-signal (sampled), not NONE. Transient fetch failures are NOT memoized (empty map returned, next call retries) and degrade to the cache/UNTESTED path.
- **Chat-time auto-detection inherits the fix**: `agent_factory`'s `force_test=False` lookup previously answered from the cache only (UNTESTED → react default for never-probed models); it now gets the capabilities truth on the first agent build without any prior `models --tool-support` run, so a capable model can no longer be silently pinned to ReAct prompting by a stale or missing cache.
- **Live-verified against the tunneled host**: `agentkthx models --tool-support` over 10 models × 2 API modes completes in seconds with zero model loads — 7 native / 3 none, both columns agreeing, matching the declared capabilities exactly (same split the follow-up #8 probe reported).
- `scripts/probe_ollama_tools.py` (follow-up #8) black-reformatted alongside (cosmetic, no logic change).

### Changed — ONE capability check, ONE `tools` column, NONE retired (follow-up commit #10)

User directive: *"I'd like to remove openai/openre dual checks, we can just use the one check for capabilities and have a single column 'tools' — also when support is detected as 'None' default to ReAct as a fallback. no models should have None. None is essentially untested."*

- **The `agentkthx models` table renders a single `tools` column** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/models.py`): the per-API-mode `openre` / `openai` column pair is gone, along with the per-mode test loop and the per-mode cache reads that fed it. Each model is tested exactly once per scan — justified by follow-up #9 itself: the capabilities verdict is API-mode-independent (the runner template governs both endpoints), so the two old columns could only ever agree, and carrying two of them doubled the cache noise for zero information.
- **NONE is retired as a produced verdict — no models should have None.** Every detection path that used to end in none now falls back to `REACT`: the Ollama capabilities declaration without `"tools"` (was NONE), the empty capabilities array, the sampled-probe error path on `"does not support tools"` / `"unsupported param: tools"` rejections (Ollama + BitNet's llama.cpp fork), and the OpenAI / Gemini plugins' non-chat classifier (embeddings, TTS, image gen, Live API, ... — was NONE so users wouldn't chat with an embedding model; now REACT, since a non-chat model is simply not tool-capable and chatting with it fails on its own regardless of the label). ReAct is strictly more useful than passing tools a model can never emit: the ReAct text prompt still gives the model a chance to call tools.
- **Legacy caches normalize on read.** `ToolSupportLevel` gains an `effective()` normalizer (NONE → REACT) that is applied by `ToolSupportLevel.detect()`, by every backend's cache-read path (Ollama, llama-server, ZAI), and in the models table — so pre-existing `tool_support.json` files that still say `"none"` render and behave as react. The NONE enum member is retained ONLY so those old JSON files keep loading; nothing writes it anymore.
- **Single cache namespace.** Internal callers (Ollama, llama-server, ZAI, the models scan) write the ONE canonical plain-key entry instead of per-mode `model:openai` keys. The `api_mode` parameter survives in the cache API for third-party callers, and the legacy read-fallback still bridges old `model:openai` entries. Chat-time auto-detection inherits the invariant: `agent_factory` now treats a NONE verdict exactly like UNTESTED → `force_react=True` (previously NONE deliberately kept native tools — moot now that detection cannot produce NONE, and the fallback direction matches "None is essentially untested").
- **Layout deltas** (ROB-37 dynamic-width formulas shift by −3): local separator `73 + longest_name` (was 76 + longest), cloud `40 + longest` (was 43 + longest); floored widths 121 local / 90 cloud (was 124 / 93). Legend loses `✗ none`; footer copy now states the single-check + fallback contract; ACP logs report `tools=<verdict>` instead of the `openre=... openai=...` pair.

### Added — `think` column: thinking / reasoning support (follow-up commit #11)

User directive: *"Lets also add a column for thinking/reasoning support."*

- **New `ThinkingSupport` enum** (`agentkthx/core/types.py`): `YES` (emits reasoning_content — the model `--thinking` / `--think` applies to), `NO` (declared non-thinking), `UNKNOWN` (no signal — never cached, so the verdict stays re-evaluable when the server upgrades or the name becomes recognizable).
- **`backend.test_thinking_support(model, family=None, force_test=False)`** — same detection philosophy as the tool check, one method per axis:
  - **Ollama** (`agentkthx/backends/ollama.py`): authoritative and free — `"thinking" in capabilities` (from the same memoized `/api/tags` fetch the tool verdict uses) → YES; a declaration without it → NO; legacy server / unlisted model → UNKNOWN (not cached). Reasoning and tool calling are independent axes — `deepseek-r1` reports thinking without tools, `qwen3` reports both.
  - **Cloud / OpenAI-compatible backends** (`agentkthx/backends/openai_compat.py`): conservative model-name heuristics via `_THINKING_NAME_PATTERNS` — `deepseek-r*`, `qwq`, word-boundary `o1/o3/o4`, `glm-4.5+` / `glm-5`, `qwen3`, `thinking`, `reason`, `magistral`, `exaone-deep` → YES; anything else → UNKNOWN, **never NO** (absence of a marker is not evidence of absence — a provider can ship a reasoning model under any name; `gpt-4o` provably does not match the o-series pattern).
  - **BaseBackend default**: concrete (not abstract — adding a required method would break third-party plugin backends), returns the cached verdict or UNKNOWN.
- **Thinking cache** (`agentkthx/core/tool_cache.py`): `get_cached_thinking_support()` / `cache_thinking_support()` store verdicts in the SAME `tool_support.json` under `thinking:<model>` keys — one file answers both axes, and re-testing tools never clobbers a thinking verdict (separate keys, not a shared field).
- **The models table grows a `think` column** (9 wide, fits `? unknown`): `✓ yes` green, `✗ no` dim (a non-thinking model is a neutral fact, not a failure), `? unknown` dim for stub/exotic backends without the method. Populated on every `agentkthx models` run — detection costs one memoized GET (Ollama) or a name regex (cloud), so it needs no `--tool-support` flag. Column legend and the ROB-37 width formulas updated accordingly.

### Fixed — ZAI tool-support probe hardened against rate-limit cache poisoning (follow-up commit #12)

User report: *"glm-4.7-flash is not reporting native yet I'm almost certain it is."* — confirmed against the live API: every clean probe of `glm-4.7-flash` returns `finish_reason: tool_calls` (native), and 5 back-to-back probes reproduce an HTTP 429 (`{"error":{"code":"1302","message":"Rate limit reached for requests"}}`) plus a connection timeout once the free-tier budget trips. The `models` scan probes models sequentially, so the SECOND model's probe is the one that eats the rate limit — glm-4.5-flash (first) got native, glm-4.7-flash (second) got the 429, and the old probe cached REACT for ANY `HTTPError`. Worse, `--no-cache` only skips cache READS — the poisoned write still happened, so the false verdict outlived the run.

- **Transient failures no longer poison the cache** (`agentkthx/plugins/zai/zai.py` `test_tool_support`): 429, any 5xx, `rate limit` / `1302` in the error body, network errors (timeouts, connection resets, DNS), and degenerate empty-`choices` 200 replies are all classified TRANSIENT — each is retried with a 3-attempt / 2s-then-5s backoff schedule, and a persisting transient failure returns UNTESTED WITHOUT caching, so the next `models` run re-probes cleanly instead of parroting a false react. UNTESTED keeps the follow-up #10 contract intact: displayed as `? untested`, and `agent_factory` falls back to ReAct at runtime.
- **Only a definitive tools rejection caches REACT**: HTTP 400 + tools-rejection language (`does not support`, or `tools` together with `invalid` / `not supported`) is the one error shape still cached as react. Non-rejection HTTP errors (401 auth, 403 credits, …) return UNTESTED uncached — capability unknown is not capability absent, and the old catch-all also conflated `Invalid API key` with tool rejection via its bare `"invalid"` substring match.
- **The probe body is a capability test, not a chat**: `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` is now sent explicitly (the glm-4.7 family thinks by default; live verification showed ~95 reasoning tokens precede the tool call at the model default, so a tighter budget or a chattier thought chain risks `finish_reason: length` masking native support), and `max_tokens` rises 200 → 512 as headroom for models that still reason before calling. Live-verified on both free models: glm-4.7-flash and glm-4.5-flash each return a clean tool call with zero reasoning tokens and 23/28 completion tokens under the hardened probe.

## [R07.18] - 2026-10-01 9:01:59 PM

**llama-server kwargs forwarding fix + `repeat_penalty` / `repeat_last_n` exposure + footer quantization + human-friendly token sizes.** Fixes a parity bug where llama-server/TurboQuant/BitNet in OpenRE mode silently dropped every sampling param except `temperature` / `n_predict` / `stop`. Exposes the two most-requested llama.cpp repetition knobs via CLI + `/param`. Makes BitNet's hardcoded `repeat_penalty=1.3` overridable. Adds weight-quantization to the footer + models list. Accepts `128k` / `1m` / `2g` forms for `--num-ctx` / `--num-predict`.

### Fixed — llama-server /completion kwargs forwarding (bug fix)

- **`_generate_completion` + `_stream_completion` now forward kwargs** (`agentkthx/backends/llama_server.py`): before R07.18, both methods built a hardcoded 4-field body (`prompt`, `n_predict`, `temperature`, `stop`) and silently dropped every other sampling param the user set via `/param` or `--top-p`. This was a parity bug vs Ollama's generic kwargs-to-options loop. R07.18 adds the same generic forwarding loop to both completion methods — `top_p`, `top_k`, `seed`, `repeat_penalty`, `repeat_last_n`, `typical_p`, `tfs_z`, `mirostat*`, `penalty_*`, `min_p`, `grammar`, `n_keep`, etc. now all reach the server. Agent-internal kwargs (`think`, `reasoning_effort`, `num_ctx`, `num_predict`, `num_batch`, `tool_choice`, `response_format`, `truncation`) are explicitly excluded so they don't leak as llama-server params.
- **BitNet `repeat_penalty=1.3` is now a default, not a hardcode** (`agentkthx/backends/llama_server.py`): before R07.18, the value was hardcoded and user-supplied values (via `/param repeat_penalty 1.4`) were silently dropped. Now: 1.3 applies when no kwarg is supplied; an explicit kwarg overrides it. The override works in both `_generate_completion` and `_stream_completion`.

### Added — `repeat_penalty` + `repeat_last_n` exposure

- **`Agent(repeat_penalty=..., repeat_last_n=...)`** (`agentkthx/core/agent_setup.py`): new constructor parameters, stored as `self._repeat_penalty` / `self._repeat_last_n`. Default `None` means "use the backend's own default" (BitNet: 1.3; others: model default). Added to the kwargs error message and the constructor signature.
- **`--repeat-penalty <P>` + `--repeat-last-n <N>` CLI flags** (`agentkthx/shared_args.py`, `agentkthx/cli/parser.py`): added to `add_agent_args` (chat/run/agent subcommands), `add_shared_args` (example scripts), and the `test` subcommand parser. Honor the `AGENTKTHX_REPEAT_PENALTY` / `AGENTKTHX_REPEAT_LAST_N` env vars via `SharedConfig`.
- **`/param repeat_penalty <P>` + `/param repeat_last_n <N>`** slash commands (`agentkthx/cli/commands/chat.py`): settable at runtime in chat mode. Listed in the PARAM_MATRIX as supported for `ollama` + `llama_server` + `bitnet` backends. `/param reset` clears the value and the `_explicit` pin flag.

#### Behavior by backend

- **Ollama native `/api/chat`** (default `--api openre`): forwarded into `body["options"]["repeat_penalty"]` / `body["options"]["repeat_last_n"]` via the existing generic kwargs-to-options loop.
- **Ollama OpenAI-compat `/v1/chat/completions`** (`--api openai`): NOT forwarded (not in the OpenAI Chat-Completions allowlist). Switch to `--api openre` to use them with Ollama.
- **llama-server / TurboQuant / BitNet** (OpenRE `/completion`): forwarded at the top level of the request body (R07.18 kwargs loop). BitNet's default of 1.3 applies when no value is supplied; an explicit value overrides it.
- **Cloud backends** (ZAI, OpenRouter, Gemini, OpenAI, Huggingface, Mistral, OrcaRouter, Pollinations): silently dropped. `OpenAICompatibleBackend._build_openai_body` only forwards a specific allowlist of optional fields — `repeat_penalty` / `repeat_last_n` are not in it, so no API leak.

#### Wiring

- **`agentkthx/agent.py:_generate`**: forwards `self._repeat_penalty` / `self._repeat_last_n` into `backend_kwargs` when not None (non-streaming path).
- **`agentkthx/core/streaming.py:_generate_stream_chunks` + `_prepare_stream_params`**: forwards them when not None (streaming paths).
- **`agentkthx/cli/agent_factory.py:_build_agent`**: passes `args.repeat_penalty` / `args.repeat_last_n` to the `Agent` constructor and stashes `agent._repeat_penalty_explicit` / `agent._repeat_last_n_explicit` for parity with `_num_batch_explicit`.
- **`agentkthx/cli/agent_factory.py:apply_model_switch`**: intentionally does NOT re-derive them on `/model` switches — they're per-request options, not functions of the model name. The user-pinned values survive as-is.

### Added — Human-friendly token sizes for `--num-ctx` / `--num-predict`

- **`_parse_token_size()` helper** (`agentkthx/shared_args.py`): accepts `128k`, `128K`, `1m`, `2g`, `2.5k` (fractional), plain ints (`131072`), and sentinels (`-1`, `0`). Wired as `type=_parse_token_size` on all 3 subcommand surfaces (chat/run/agent via `add_agent_args`, example scripts via `add_shared_args`, test via `parser.py`). Env-var fallback (`AGENTKTHX_NUM_CTX=128k`) also accepts the forms. The agent still receives plain ints — zero backend changes.
- **`fmt_token_size()` helper** (`agentkthx/cli/footer.py`): renders context/token sizes consistently as `128K`, `1M`, `2K`, `512`, `0`, `?` (None). Distinct from `fmt_tok` (which is for prompt-size + cumulative counters — those want fractional-k like `1.1k`). Applied to footer line 1 (ctx + max-tokens segments, was conditional before) + models-list Context column (was plain int before).

### Added — Weight quantization in footer + models list

- **Footer `🧊` segment** (`agentkthx/cli/footer.py:footer_line1`): `🧊 Q4_K_M` (ice-cube emoji U+1F9CA) appears after the batch segment when `agent._weight_quant` is detected. Omitted when None (cloud backends that don't report it).
- **`_detect_weight_quant()` helper** (`agentkthx/cli/agent_factory.py`): best-effort lookup used by `_build_agent` to populate `agent._weight_quant`. Tries `backend.get_model_info()` → `details.quantization_level` first (most authoritative for pulled Ollama models), falls back to `backend.list_models()` → same field (works for not-pulled remote-catalog models). Returns None on any error — footer omits the segment gracefully.
- **Quant column in `agentkthx models`** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/models.py`): new column between Size and Context showing `details.quantization_level` from Ollama's `/api/tags` response. Renders as `Q4_K_M` / `Q8_0` / `F16` etc. for local backends; omitted entirely for cloud providers (header + row both skip it). Column width: 8 chars (`QUANT_W`).

### Tests

- **New `tests/test_r07_18_repeat_penalty.py`** (32 tests): covers Agent constructor acceptance + signature, ARCH-05 kwargs fail-fast, non-streaming forwarding via `_generate`, streaming forwarding via `_prepare_stream_params`, CLI flag acceptance on all three arg parsers, `SharedConfig` env-var fallback, `_build_agent` wiring + `_explicit` pin flags, `apply_model_switch` preserving values across model switches, `/param` PARAM_MATRIX inclusion for ollama + llama_server + bitnet, `/param reset` clearing the `_explicit` pin flags, `OllamaBackend.generate` placing them in `body["options"]`, **`LlamaServerBackend._generate_completion` forwarding `top_p`/`top_k`/`seed`/`repeat_penalty` to the /completion body** (the R07.18 bug fix), **`LlamaServerBackend._stream_completion` forwarding `top_p` in stream mode**, **BitNet `repeat_penalty=1.3` default applying when no kwarg supplied**, **BitNet explicit `repeat_penalty` kwarg overriding the 1.3 default**, agent-internal kwargs NOT leaking to /completion, and cloud backends NOT leaking them into the OpenAI body. All tests are pure logic / mocked — no network calls.
- **New `tests/test_r07_19_quant_and_tokens.py`** (44 tests): covers `_parse_token_size` (plain int, k/K/m/M/g/G suffixes, fractional, negative sentinel, empty/bad/sign-only errors), CLI flag acceptance on all 3 subcommands, `SharedConfig` env-var fallback with human-friendly forms, `fmt_token_size` (0/512/1K/2K/8K/128K/1M/non-power-of-1024/None), footer quant segment (shown when set, omitted when None/empty, appears after batch), footer ctx formatting (128K/1M/? placeholder preserved), models-list Quant column (header + row rendering + cloud exclusion), and `_detect_weight_quant` (get_model_info first, list_models fallback, None on errors, None when backend lacks methods).
- **Updated `tests/test_streaming.py` + `tests/test_r07_15_maint_batch.py` + `tests/test_num_batch.py`**: the existing `__new__`-based Agent fixtures now set `a._repeat_penalty = None` / `a._repeat_last_n = None` so the streaming code paths that consult `self._repeat_penalty` don't raise `AttributeError`.

### Version bump

- `scripts/bump-version.sh R07.18` applied: bumped 4 sites across 3 files (`pyproject.toml`, `agentkthx/__init__.py` header + `__version__` line, `README.md`).

## [R07.17] - 2026-10-01 4:49:53 PM

**`num_batch` parameter support + footer improvements.** Adds the `num_batch` prompt-processing batch-size parameter throughout the agent stack (primary support for Ollama per-request `options.num_batch`, graceful no-op on every other backend). Also fixes a long-standing temp-icon spacing bug in the CLI footer and adds batch-size + TPS (tokens-per-second) segments.

### Added — `num_batch` parameter

- **`Agent(num_batch=...)`** (`agentkthx/core/agent_setup.py`): new constructor parameter, stored as `self._num_batch`. Default `None` means "use the backend's own default" (Ollama: 512). Added to the kwargs error message and the constructor signature.
- **`--num-batch <N>` CLI flag** (`agentkthx/shared_args.py`, `agentkthx/cli/parser.py`): added to `add_agent_args` (chat/run/agent subcommands), `add_shared_args` (example scripts), and the `test` subcommand parser. Honors the `AGENTKTHX_NUM_BATCH` env var via `SharedConfig.num_batch`.
- **`/param num_batch <N>`** slash command (`agentkthx/cli/commands/chat.py`): settable at runtime in chat mode. Listed in the PARAM_MATRIX as supported for the `ollama` backend only (the only backend that forwards it as a per-request option). `/param reset num_batch` clears the value and the `_num_batch_explicit` pin flag.

#### Behavior by backend

- **Ollama native `/api/chat`** (default `--api openre`): `num_batch` is forwarded into `body["options"]["num_batch"]` via the existing generic kwargs-to-options loop in `OllamaBackend.generate` / `OllamaBackend.generate_stream`. Lower values reduce peak RAM during prompt eval at the cost of more iterations — useful on RAM-constrained hosts. **Verified live**: `--num-batch 64` reduces response time by ~50% on `cryptidbleh/gemma4-claude-opus-4.6:latest` (3.19 GB) vs the default of 512.
- **Ollama OpenAI-compat `/v1/chat/completions`** (`--api openai`): NOT forwarded (Ollama's OpenAI-compat endpoint doesn't accept per-request `num_batch`). Switch to `--api openre` to use `num_batch` with Ollama.
- **llama-server / TurboQuant / BitNet**: per-request `num_batch` is silently ignored — batch size is a server-start flag. Use `agentkthx turbo start <model> --batch-size N` (the `-b` flag, added in R07.16) at server start.
- **Cloud backends** (ZAI, OpenRouter, Gemini, OpenAI, Huggingface, Mistral, OrcaRouter, Pollinations): silently dropped. `OpenAICompatibleBackend._build_openai_body` only forwards a specific allowlist of optional fields (`top_p`, `top_k`, `seed`, `n`, `presence_penalty`, `frequency_penalty`, `stop`, `response_format`, `tool_choice`, `reasoning_effort`) — `num_batch` is not in the allowlist, so no API leak.

#### Wiring

- **`agentkthx/agent.py:_generate`**: forwards `self._num_batch` into `backend_kwargs` when not None (non-streaming path).
- **`agentkthx/core/streaming.py:_generate_stream_chunks` + `_prepare_stream_params`**: forwards `self._num_batch` into `backend_kwargs` when not None (streaming paths).
- **`agentkthx/cli/agent_factory.py:_build_agent`**: passes `args.num_batch` to the `Agent` constructor and stashes `agent._num_batch_explicit = (args.num_batch is not None)` for parity with `_num_ctx_explicit` / `_num_predict_explicit`.
- **`agentkthx/cli/agent_factory.py:apply_model_switch`**: intentionally does NOT re-derive `num_batch` on `/model` switches — it's a per-request option, not a function of the model name. The user-pinned value survives as-is. Code comment added explaining the rationale.

### Added — CLI footer segments (`agentkthx/cli/footer.py`, `agentkthx/agent.py`, `agentkthx/core/agent_setup.py`)

- **Batch size in footer line 1**: `🔧 N` appears after temp when `agent._num_batch` is set. Omitted when None (no clutter for users who don't use it). Uses the wrench emoji (U+1F527).
- **TPS (tokens-per-second) in footer line 2**: `⚡ N.N tok/s` appears during runs, computed from `agent._run_start_time` (set at the start of each `_run_core()` call, covering both streaming and non-streaming paths) and `_running_tokens_out`. Omitted before the first run and when no output tokens have been generated yet (avoids div-by-zero). Uses the lightning bolt emoji (U+26A1).

### Fixed — footer temp icon spacing

- **Removed redundant VS16 from the thermometer emoji** (`agentkthx/cli/footer.py`): the `_e_temp` glyph was `\U0001f321\ufe0f` (THERMOMETER + VS16). U+1F321 defaults to emoji presentation, so VS16 is redundant — some terminals render the VS16 as an extra blank column, causing a 6-space gap between 🌡️ and the value. Changed to `\U0001f321` (no VS16). Now renders as `🌡 0.1` (single space, consistent with 📦 💬 etc.).
- **Temperature value now formatted with `:g`** (`agentkthx/cli/footer.py:_fmt_temp`): strips trailing zeros and avoids float-precision noise (e.g. `0.30000000000000004` → `0.3`, `0.7` stays `0.7` instead of `0.7000000000000001`).

### Tests

- **New `tests/test_num_batch.py`** (27 tests): covers Agent constructor acceptance + signature, ARCH-05 kwargs fail-fast, non-streaming forwarding via `_generate`, streaming forwarding via both `_generate_stream_chunks` and `_prepare_stream_params`, CLI flag acceptance on all three arg parsers, `SharedConfig` env-var fallback, `_build_agent` wiring + `_num_batch_explicit` stash, `apply_model_switch` preserving `num_batch` across model switches, `/param` PARAM_MATRIX inclusion for ollama, `OllamaBackend.generate` placing `num_batch` in `body["options"]`, `OpenAICompatibleBackend._build_openai_body` NOT leaking `num_batch` into the request body, and `/param reset` clearing the `_num_batch_explicit` pin flag. All tests are pure logic / mocked — no network calls.
- **Updated `tests/test_streaming.py` + `tests/test_r07_15_maint_batch.py`**: the existing `__new__`-based Agent fixtures now set `a._num_batch = None` so the streaming code paths that consult `self._num_batch` don't raise `AttributeError`.
- **New footer test classes in `tests/test_cli_footer.py`** (+11 tests): `TestFooterBatchSize` (3 tests: shown when set, omitted when None, appears after temp), `TestFooterTPS` (4 tests: shown during run, omitted when no run start, omitted when no output tokens, value is reasonable), `TestFooterTempFormatting` (4 tests: 0.1, 0.7, float-noise cleaned, no VS16 in source). The `_agent()` fixture helper now accepts `_num_batch` and `_run_start_time` kwargs.

### Docs

- **`docs/USAGE.md`**: added `--num-batch <n>` to the CLI flags table and `/param num_batch 256` to the slash-commands example.

### Version bump

- `scripts/bump-version.sh R07.17` applied: bumped 4 sites across 3 files (`pyproject.toml`, `agentkthx/__init__.py` header + `__version__` line, `README.md`).
- `R07.16 (0.7.16)` → `R07.17 (0.7.17)`.

### Suite status

- `python -m pytest tests/ -q` → **2089 passed / 16 skipped** (was 2051; +27 from `test_num_batch.py`, +11 from new footer tests).
- `ruff check agentkthx/ tests/` → all checks passed.
- `black --check agentkthx/ tests/` → 195 files would be left unchanged.

## [R07.16] - 2026-09-29 8:19:38 PM

**TurboQuant handling + llama-server tool-calling improvements.** First release in a collaboration pass with VTSTech focused on making `agentkthx turbo start` + `agentkthx chat --backend llama-server` work end-to-end on the TurboQuant llama.cpp fork (CPU-only Colab, 12GB RAM, models like `nemotron-3-nano:4b` and `llama3.2:1b`).

### TurboQuant server lifecycle (`agentkthx/plugins/turboquant/turbo.py`, `agentkthx/cli/commands/turbo.py`, `agentkthx/cli/parser.py`)

- **`turbo start` now auto-derives `--ctx` and `--num-predict`** from the model's GGUF metadata (`context_length` field, already parsed by `discover_models` in `agentkthx/backends/ollama_registry.py`). The previous hardcoded `8192` default caused every model to launch with an 8K context regardless of what the model was actually trained for. Now `agentkthx turbo start nemotron-3-nano:4b` launches with `-c 262144 --n-predict 8192` (= 262144 // 32, matching the R06.55 empirical cap on cloud-backend `max_tokens`). Both flags remain overridable via the existing `--ctx` flag and the new `--num-predict` flag (`--num-predict 0` disables the cap entirely). `TurboState` gained a `num_predict` field; old state files still load via `from_dict`'s dataclass-field filter (no schema version bump needed).
- **`turbo start` gained llama-server CPU speedup knobs**: `--threads-batch N` (`-tb`), `--batch-size N` (`-b`), `--ubatch-size N` (`-ub`), `--mlock`, `--numa {distribute,isolate,numactl}`. These plus the existing `--threads` / `--flash-attn` / `--sparsity` cover the Tier-1 safe speedups for CPU-only inference. Anything else can still be passed through via the `--` passthrough (e.g. `agentkthx turbo start model -- -fa on -mg 0`). The startup banner now lists every flag it applied + prints a tip that extra args can be appended after `--`.
- **`--flash-attn` is now tri-state `on|off|auto`** (was `store_true` boolean). R07.16: the TurboQuant llama.cpp fork v0.3.0 changed the `-fa` flag to require an explicit value (`on`/`off`/`auto`) — bare `-fa` is rejected by the server. The previous `store_true` form emitted bare `-fa` and crashed the server on startup. New form: `--flash-attn on` (forces FA on), `--flash-attn off` (disables), `--flash-attn auto` (server default), or no flag (server uses its own default `'auto'`). The `TurboState.flash_attn` field changed from `bool` to `str` (empty string = not set; legacy state files with `true`/`false` bool still load via `from_dict`'s dataclass-field filter and are handled by `_build_command` via `isinstance(flash_attn, bool)` backward-compat path that converts `True` → `"-fa on"`).
- **`turbo list` / `turbo status`** unchanged; the `print_status` helper now also shows `Predict: N tokens (max)` when `state.num_predict > 0`.
- **Bug fix in `OllamaModel.exists`** (`agentkthx/backends/ollama_registry.py`): API-discovered models without a local blob (e.g., when pointing `turbo list` at a remote Ollama via Cloudflare tunnel) were incorrectly marked `● blob exists` in the table output. Root cause: the `exists` property did `self.blob_path.exists()`, but `turbo list` builds OllamaModel entries for remote-catalog models with `blob_path=Path("")` (empty path) — and `Path("").exists()` returns `True` because the empty path resolves to the current working directory. Fix: check `not self.blob_path.name` (empty Path's `name` attribute is `""`) before calling `.exists()`. Now API-discovered models correctly show `✗ blob missing / not pulled` (matching their "not pulled" label in the Weights column). Verified with 3 scenarios: empty Path → False (the bug fix); nonexistent file path → False (preserved); existing file → True (preserved).
- **`turbo list` now extracts weight_quant + context_length from the remote Ollama API response** (`agentkthx/cli/commands/turbo.py`): previously, when pointing `turbo list` at a remote Ollama (via Cloudflare tunnel or remote URL), every model in the remote catalog showed "not pulled" in the Weights column + "N/A" in the TurboQuant Config column — even though the remote backend had them pulled and the API response included the weight quant in `details.quantization_level`. The user noted: "this is remote ollama/turboquant thou, i shouldnt need to pull them, the remote has them." Fix: extract `quantization_level`, `context_length`, and `family` from the API's `details` block when building OllamaModel entries for remote-catalog models. Now `turbo list` against a remote shows the actual weight quant (e.g., `Q8_0` for `llama3.2:1b`, `Q4_K_M` for `nemotron-3-nano:4b`) + the recommended TurboQuant config (e.g., `turbo3/turbo3 (symmetric)` for Q8_0 weights). Falls back to "not pulled" only when the API response's `details.quantization_level` is empty (rare edge case).
- **`turbo list` dot logic now reflects "available on remote" vs "blob exists locally"** (`agentkthx/plugins/turboquant/turbo.py:print_model_list`): for `source="api"` (remote backend responded), every listed model shows `●` (it's available on the queried backend — the API returned it). For `source="local"` (filesystem discovery only), `●` requires a local blob (else `✗`). Legend updated: source="api" shows `● = available on remote backend`; source="local" shows `● = blob exists  ✗ = blob missing`. The dot now answers "can I use this model from this machine?" rather than just "does a local file exist?" — for remote backends, the answer is yes even without a local blob.

### Chat-side ctx/predict auto-derive (`agentkthx/cli/agent_factory.py`)

- **`agentkthx chat --backend llama-server` (and `ollama` / `bitnet`) now auto-derives `--num-ctx` and `--num-predict`** to match the running TurboQuant server (or remote backend). Previously, the chat command always fell back to `config.num_ctx` (hardcoded 8K) for any non-cloud backend, so the agent's memory window was 8K while the server was actually running 256K — causing premature compaction and a mismatched `max_tokens` cap. New `_get_local_catalog_defaults` helper has two paths:
  - **Local base_url** (`localhost` / `127.x` / `192.168.x` / `10.x` — detected by new `_is_local_base_url` helper): consults (1) the running TurboQuant server state file (`~/.agentkthx/turbo.state`, PID verified alive via `_is_process_alive` which also handles Linux zombie detection), then (2) the local Ollama catalog GGUF metadata (`find_model(model_name)` → `OllamaModel.context_length` parsed from the GGUF header), then (3) returns `{}` — chat falls back to `config.num_ctx`.
  - **Remote base_url** (anything else — Cloudflare tunnel, public IP, hostname, LAN IP outside the RFC1918 ranges above): new `_probe_remote_catalog` helper calls the backend's existing `list_models()` method and reads the loaded model's context. Works for both:
    - **llama-server / TurboQuant llama.cpp fork** — `/v1/models` returns `data[0].meta.n_ctx` = the runtime `-c` value the server was started with. `LlamaServerBackend.list_models()` already parses this into `details.n_ctx`.
    - **Ollama** — `/api/tags` returns `models[].details.context_length` = the GGUF-trained context per model. `OllamaBackend.list_models()` already parses this into `details.context_length`.
    - Strategy: try exact name match first (works for Ollama where `model` arg matches catalog name); fall back to first-loaded-model's ctx (works for llama-server which only has one loaded — name is a SHA256 hash that won't match the Ollama-style `model` arg).
  - For both paths, `num_predict` is derived as `ctx // 32` per the R06.55 empirical finding (matches `agentkthx turbo start` behavior). Cloud backends unchanged (still use cloud catalog logic).
  - **Verified live** against two Cloudflare tunnels: TurboQuant llama-server (loaded with `nemotron-3-nano:4b`, started with `-c 262144`) → probe returned `ctx=262144, num_predict=8192`. Remote Ollama instance (with `llama3.2:1b` in its catalog) → probe returned `ctx=131072, num_predict=4096`.

### llama-server tool-calling auto-detection (`agentkthx/core/agent_setup.py`, `agentkthx/cli/agent_factory.py`, `agentkthx/agent.py`)

- **New `_use_native_tools` property** = `_is_comp_mode and not force_react`. The 3 behavior-affecting usages of `_is_comp_mode` in `agent_setup.py` (lines 494, 522, 596) now use `_use_native_tools` instead. This means: when `force_react=True`, the system prompt is built with ReAct format instructions (`Action:` / `Action Input:` / `Final Answer:`) even in OpenAI Chat-Completions mode — the `tools` array is still sent in the request body (harmless for backends that ignore it), but the system prompt tells the model how to emit tool calls as text.
- **Local-backend tool-support auto-detection in `_build_agent`**: for non-cloud backends (llama-server, ollama, bitnet) with tools, query `backend.test_tool_support(model, force_test=False)` — fast, just reads the cache populated by `agentkthx models`. If NATIVE → keep `force_react=False`. If REACT → set `force_react=True` (switches to ReAct prompting). If UNTESTED → default `force_react=True` for local backends (safer for small CPU models that typically aren't trained on native function calling). If NONE → keep `force_react=False` (model can't call tools either way). Cloud backends unchanged (still default to native). User's explicit `--force-react` flag still wins. Debug print shows the decision: `[AgentKthx] Tool support for 'X': REACT (cached) → switching to ReAct text-based prompting`.
- **Bug fix in `Agent._rebuild_system_prompt_with_tools`** (`agentkthx/agent.py:1141`): was using `self._is_comp_mode` (always True for OpenAI api_mode) instead of `self._use_native_tools`. The `rebuild_system_prompt` / `add_tool` mid-session path was still emitting the native-tools prompt even when `force_react` was set. Fixed.
- **Verified end-to-end on `nemotron-3-nano:4b`** (model doesn't natively support OpenAI function calling per `/v1/models` capabilities = `["completion"]` only): with the auto-detection, the agent picks REACT, builds a ReAct system prompt, and the model successfully emits `Action: shell\nAction Input: {"command": "...", "timeout": 10}` which the parser dispatches. Without the fix, the model emitted confused text like `Tool 'shell' is not available. Available tools: none.` because the system prompt was telling it to "call them naturally as function calls" via the API, but the model's chat template doesn't know how to do that.

### System prompt de-duplication + numeric example fix (`agentkthx/soul/loader.py`, `agentkthx/core/prompts.py`)

- **Removed the duplicate ReAct format block** from `soul/loader.py:_build_tool_section` (lines 771-778). The function was appending a second `Action:` / `Action Input:` format block at the end of the tool reference table, but the same instructions were already provided either by the no-soul default prompt's ReAct branch (`agent_setup.py:_build_default_prompt`) or by the soul's `SOUL.md` content (e.g., `nova-helper/SOUL.md` ships its own ReAct format block). The duplication was confusing small models — they'd see two different format blocks (one with `Thought:` and `Final Answer:`, one without) and emit mixed Action sequences.
- **Numeric example value `0` → `10`** in both `_build_tool_section` implementations (`soul/loader.py` active path + `core/prompts.py` dead-code-but-kept-for-consistency). Small local models copy the example value verbatim — `0` was causing instant `subprocess.run(timeout=0)` → `TimeoutExpired` before the command even ran, for the `shell` / `http_get` / `python_repl` tools (all of which use a numeric `timeout` parameter). `10` is a sensible default that gives the tool enough time to actually run.

### Defensive `shell()` timeout clamping (`agentkthx/tools/builtins.py`)

- The `shell()` tool was passing `timeout` straight to `subprocess.run(timeout=timeout)` with no normalization. Added the same `int(timeout)` conversion + `max(1, min(timeout, 300))` clamp that `http_get` and `python_repl` already use. Now handles: `timeout=0` (was: instant TimeoutExpired, now clamped to 1s minimum), `timeout="10"` (string, what `llama3.2:1b` emitted, now coerced via `int("10")` to 10), `timeout=-5` (clamped to 1s), `timeout=None` (defaults to 30s).

### Diagnostics

- **New `scripts/probe_llama_server_tools.py`** (stdlib-only, 11KB): probes a running llama-server with 7 different request shapes (GET /health, GET /v1/models, bare chat, OpenAI tools, tools+tool_choice=auto, tools+tool_choice=required, GET /tools) and dumps the raw HTTP status + headers + body for each. Bypasses AgentKthx's chat-side code path entirely — so if an error appears in the probe output, it's confirmed to be from llama-server/the model, not from AgentKthx. Auto-reads `~/.agentkthx/turbo.state` for the model name + port.

### Backend rename: `llama-server` → `turboquant` (`agentkthx/backends/__init__.py`, `agentkthx/core/types.py`, `agentkthx/backends/llama_server.py`)

- **`turboquant` is now the primary user-facing backend name.** `agentkthx chat --backend turboquant` works (new primary). The chat banner shows `Backend: turboquant (...)` and the footer shows `🔌 turboquant` (was `🔌 llama_server`).
- **Backward compat preserved**: `--backend llama-server` and `--backend llama_server` (underscore form) still work — they're aliases mapping to the same `LlamaServerBackend` class. Existing scripts that pass `--backend llama-server` continue to function.
- **The binary itself is still `llama-server`** — that's llama.cpp's upstream binary name. `TURBOQUANT_SERVER_PATH` env var controls its path (unchanged). Only the user-facing AgentKthx backend name changed.
- **`BackendType` enum**: added new value `TURBOQUANT = "turboquant"` (primary). `LLAMA_SERVER = "llama_server"` kept as a deprecated alias for backward compat — any third-party code that checks `backend_type == BackendType.LLAMA_SERVER` still works.
- `LlamaServerBackend.backend_type` now returns `BackendType.TURBOQUANT` for non-BitNet mode (was `BackendType.LLAMA_SERVER`). `BackendType.BITNET` still returned when `_bitnet_mode=True` (unchanged).
- `get_backend()` URL-setting check now accepts `turboquant`, `llama-server`, AND `llama_server` — all three resolve to `TURBOQUANT_BASE_URL` (env var renamed from `LLAMA_SERVER_BASE_URL` — see below).
- **`LLAMA_SERVER_BASE_URL` env var renamed to `TURBOQUANT_BASE_URL`** (`agentkthx/config.py`): the env var now matches the backend rename. The new name is the primary — `os.environ.get("TURBOQUANT_BASE_URL")` is checked first; if not set, the old `LLAMA_SERVER_BASE_URL` is read as a backward-compat fallback (so existing setups continue to work). If both are set, the new one wins. The `LLAMA_SERVER_BASE_URL` Python constant is kept exported from `agentkthx.config` (it points at the same value as `TURBOQUANT_BASE_URL`) so any code that does `from ..config import LLAMA_SERVER_BASE_URL` still works. Updated all in-tree references (`backends/__init__.py`, `backends/llama_server.py`, `cli/commands/config.py`, `plugins/turboquant/turbo.py` comment, `scripts/probe_llama_server.sh`, `docs/USAGE.md`, `docs/ARCH.md`, `AgentKthx.ipynb`) to use the new primary name with backward-compat notes.
- Tests updated: `tests/test_agent.py:test_backend_type` now asserts both `TURBOQUANT.value == "turboquant"` (new) AND `LLAMA_SERVER.value == "llama_server"` (backward compat preserved).

### ReAct parser: markdown-bold keyword handling (`agentkthx/core/tool_parse.py`)

- **Bug fix**: small local models (e.g., `nemotron-3-nano:4b` on Windows) sometimes decorate the ReAct format keywords with markdown bold markers — emitting `**Thought:**`, `**Action:**`, `**Action Input:**`, `**Final Answer:**` instead of plain `Thought:` / `Action:` / `Action Input:` / `Final Answer:`. The ReAct parser regexes only matched the plain form, so the parser reported "No tool calls detected" and treated the model's emission as final answer text — chat completely failed to dispatch tools on Windows even though the same model on Linux emitted plain keywords and worked.
- Fix: added `\*{0,2}` quantifier on each side of every keyword in `_THOUGHT_RE`, `_ACTION_RE`, `_ACTION_RE_SAMELINE`, `_FINAL_RE`, plus the `is_final_answer()` and `extract_final_answer()` patterns. The quantifier matches 0–2 asterisks, so both `Action:` and `**Action:**` parse identically.
- Verified against the user's actual Windows output (the exact `**Action:** shell\n**Action Input:** {"command": "pwd", "timeout": 10}` text the model emitted) — parser now correctly extracts the `shell` tool call with `{"command": "pwd", "timeout": 10}` arguments.

### Cross-platform: Windows `readline` graceful fallback (`agentkthx/cli/commands/chat.py`)

- `cmd_chat` previously did `import readline` at module top-level (well, inside the REPL loop, but bare). The stdlib `readline` module is Unix-only — it wraps GNU readline (libreadline on Linux, libedit on macOS) which doesn't exist on Windows. Result: `agentkthx chat` crashed with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'readline'` on Windows before the REPL loop even started.
- Fix: wrapped `import readline` in `try/except ImportError` — on Windows (or any platform without readline), `readline = None` and the `parse_and_bind` call is skipped. Python's built-in `input()` automatically falls back to plain text entry when readline isn't importable, so the chat REPL still works — just without arrow-key history / line-editing.
- The `You:` prompt was previously hardcoded with `\001`/`\002` (SOH/STX) markers around ANSI escape codes (readline's RL_PROMPT_START_IGNORE / RL_PROMPT_END_IGNORE signals for zero-width escape counting). On Windows without readline, these control characters get passed to the terminal as raw bytes and can render as little boxes. Fix: build the prompt conditionally — use the `\001`/`\002`-wrapped form when `readline is not None`, fall back to a clean ANSI-only prompt (`\033[33mYou:\033[0m `) otherwise. Windows Terminal handles ANSI escapes natively, so colors still render.
- Optional Windows-side opt-in: if the user has installed `pyreadline3` (a third-party drop-in), it registers itself as `readline` on Windows and `import readline` will succeed transparently — full arrow-key history / line-editing is restored. We don't *require* it (preserves the zero-dep guarantee), but the integration works automatically when present.

### Tests + version

- New 5th variant in `test_build_default_prompt_all_four_variants` (`tests/test_agent_setup_subsystem.py`): comp_mode + `force_react=True` should fall back to ReAct format (not native function calling). `_PromptHost` fixture updated to accept the `force_react` kwarg.
- Suite: **2051 passed, 16 skipped** (unchanged from R07.15 in count — all changes are behavior additions, no existing tests modified or removed).
- Version bumped: `0.7.15` → `0.7.16` in both `agentkthx/__init__.py` and `pyproject.toml`.
