## [R07.15] - 2026-09-29 2:14:18 PM

**Push history (github.com/VTSTech/AgentKthx, branch `main`):** `30cbe4f` — this release (six-finding closure batch) · `4e1555e` — intra-release amendment 1 (`openrouter/free` free-models listing) · `7579e3c` — intra-release amendment 2 (agent-mode tool-call counter + audit header sync) · `7f59a8d` — docs-only amendment 3 (push-history annotations) · `53916a5` — intra-release amendment 4 (four-finding closure batch: TEST-06, FEAT-02, ROB-11, ROB-22) · `ea98951` — intra-release amendment 5 (chat per-response stats + dynamic `--backend` help) · `8b86fb7` — intra-release amendment 6 (pyyaml dev extra: CI-pinning tests run in CI) · `00ad56a` — ruff config key migration (`select` → `lint.select`) · `8d0b8c7` — lint burn-down (ruff+black across the tree, lint job promoted to REQUIRED). All nine commits are titled "R07.15".

**Six-finding closure batch — five Maintainability findings + one Performance finding closed (MAINT-07, MAINT-08, MAINT-10, MAINT-15, MAINT-19, PERF-03), 53 new tests, zero regressions.** This is the second audit-closure-style release in a row: no new features, no new surface — every change shrinks the register or makes an existing path easier to extend. The two Medium Maintainability findings are the headline pair: `Agent._generate_stream` (MAINT-08, the largest single extraction since the R07.00 modularization) and the LLM agent router's injectable prompt (MAINT-10, the only finding in this batch with a security dimension). The four Low findings close small-but-real fragility: Qwen family-config indirection (MAINT-07), a per-instance-only SQLite write lock (MAINT-15), three cloud backends re-fetching their model catalogs once per instance instead of once per process (MAINT-19), and DuckDuckGo parsing that leaned on four brittle regexes (PERF-03 — closed with the corrected stdlib `html.parser` recommendation the R07.14 release documented). Suite: 1935 → **1988 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+53 tests in `tests/test_r07_15_maint_batch.py`).

### Maintainability

- **MAINT-08 CLOSED — `_generate_stream` extracted along its two natural seams** (`core/streaming.py`): the 354-line method with 3 inline closures, 3 accumulator collections, and stdout writes woven through 5-level `try/except/finally` nesting is now ~90 lines of orchestration. `StreamAccumulator` owns the DATA — content/reasoning delta merging, OpenAI's split-across-chunks tool_call format (index-keyed slots: first chunk carries `id` + `function.name`, later chunks append partial `function.arguments` JSON), and a `finalize()` producing the exact same dict shape as `_generate()`, including the reasoning→content promotion for models that answer in `reasoning_content` and the `_raw_arguments` fallback for malformed cross-chunk JSON (behavior-identical, kept silent per the documented ROB-13-style gap). `StreamRenderer` owns the PRESENTATION — the once-per-step `AgentKthx:` prefix, the `reasoning:` panel with its 4-space indent tracking, the reasoning→content transition newline — and accepts an optional `out` stream, so tests capture rendered bytes directly instead of redirecting `sys.stdout` (pytest `capsys` keeps working via write-time stdout resolution). Param resolution moved to a `_prepare_stream_params()` helper. Rendered bytes and return-dict shape are unchanged — pinned by the existing `test_streaming.py` suite plus 17 new MAINT-08 tests (8 accumulator, 6 renderer, 3 end-to-end through `_generate_stream`). FEAT-06 (streaming tool-call argument deltas) can now build on `StreamAccumulator` without touching rendering.
- **MAINT-10 CLOSED — agent router prompt hardened against injection** (`orchestrator.py`): `_select_agent_with_llm` used to interpolate agent descriptions and the raw task straight into an f-string and then substring-match agent names ANYWHERE in the reply — a hostile description (`AgentCard` descriptions can come from external sources such as ACP discovery) or a prompt-injected task could steer the router to the wrong agent, and a name that was a substring of another (`coder` vs `coder2`) matched by dict order. The hardened flow implements all three of the finding's recommendations: (1) descriptions are wrapped in `<agent name="...">...</agent>` XML blocks with `html.escape()` applied to name, description AND task, so injected markup stays inert DATA; (2) a system message tells the router the blocks are data to classify, not instructions to follow; (3) the reply is validated STRICTLY — after repeatedly stripping surrounding whitespace/quotes/punctuation it must EQUAL a registered agent name (case-insensitive), never a substring — and an invalid reply triggers exactly ONE re-prompt restating the valid names at temperature 0, after which the deterministic keyword scorer takes over (preserving the old first-agent ultimate fallback). Pinned by 8 new tests, including injection attempts that the old substring scan would have honored.
- **MAINT-07 CLOSED — model family resolution made deterministic, no substring guessing between detect and config** (`core/model_family_config.py`): `detect_family()` returned strings like `qwen2.5` that `FAMILY_CONFIGS` didn't hold, so `get_family_config()` silently resolved them through its substring partial match — a 2-step indirection the finding warned could break new variants into wrong stop tokens / temperature with no error. Every detectable family now resolves through a documented path: explicit `qwen2.5` config entry (a clone of the Qwen2 ChatML template — identical start/stop tokens, temperature, tool format — so only the reported family name changed), a commented `_FAMILY_ALIASES` map (now covering `qwen3.5`→`qwen35`, bare `qwen`→`qwen2`, all `llama3.x`→`llama`, `gemma2`→`gemma3`, `bitnet`→`llama`), and a `_DEFAULT_THROUGH_FAMILIES` set pinning which families intentionally fall through to the neutral default. The partial-match fallback sorts keys LONGEST-first so the explicit `qwen2.5` entry can no longer lose to `qwen2` by dict insertion order, and the detection list is extracted to an ordered, commented `_DETECT_FAMILIES` constant (most-specific prefix first: `qwen2.5` before `qwen2`, `deepseek-r1` before `deepseek`). The finding's named test is present in spirit: a sweep asserting `get_family_config(detect_family(m))` agrees for every detectable family, plus a detect/get agreement pin for `qwen2.5-coder` (9 MAINT-07 tests total). One behavioral change, intended and tested: after an `apply_model_switch` to `qwen2.5:0.5b`, `model_config.family` now reports `qwen2.5` (the actual detected family) instead of the old silently-resolved `qwen2` — template values identical (`tests/test_model_switch_context.py` updated).
- **MAINT-15 CLOSED — PersistentMemory write lock is per-DB-path, not per-instance** (`core/persistent_memory.py`): ROB-03 (R07.05) put a `threading.Lock` around every SQLite write, but the lock lived ON THE INSTANCE — two `PersistentMemory` handles on the SAME database file (two sessions in one process, Orchestrator parallel mode with per-agent memory, a tool thread constructing its own handle) each had their own lock, so the cross-instance race the lock was meant to prevent could still happen; SQLite's file locking serializes at the OS level, but concurrent `execute()` calls still tripped `busy_timeout` errors. A module-level `WeakValueDictionary` registry now hands out ONE lock per `realpath`-normalized database path: instances hold the only strong reference via `self._write_lock`, entries vanish when the last handle for a path is garbage-collected (no unbounded growth, no teardown), and a guard lock makes get-or-create atomic. Pinned by tests that construct two handles on one path and assert a shared lock — plus a distinct-path test proving locks are NOT over-shared.
- **MAINT-19 CLOSED — `list_models` cache is genuinely class-level in all three caching cloud backends** (`plugins/openai/openai.py`, `plugins/openrouter/openrouter.py`, `plugins/huggingface/huggingface.py`): the cache attributes were declared at class level but WRITTEN via `self._model_cache = ...`, which silently created a per-instance shadow — and since the CLI's `agent_factory` builds a temp backend for model discovery plus the real one, and OpenAI/HF constructors call `list_models()` on every construction, each instance re-fetched `/models` once per instance instead of once per TTL window. Live fetches now write through `type(self)` (assigning on the actual class, keeping subclasses isolated — each gets its own attribute on first write); the static-catalog failure fallback deliberately STAYS instance-level so a network failure can't poison the shared cache for instances that might succeed after the network returns (exact pre-MAINT-19 semantics preserved for the failing instance). Pinned by tests asserting two instances share one cache after a fetch and that a fallback write does not leak into the class.

### Performance

- **PERF-03 CLOSED — `web_search` parses DuckDuckGo HTML with stdlib `html.parser`, not regexes** (`tools/builtins.py`): the block split + 4 regex patterns (`re.DOTALL | re.IGNORECASE` over both endpoints) and the lite endpoint's 2000-char forward-search snippet window are replaced by `_DDGResultParser`, one real HTML tokenizer handling BOTH endpoint layouts via class-name sets (`result__a`/`result-link` anchors, `result__snippet`/`result-snippet` snippets) — attribute order, quote style, extra whitespace, nested tags, and character references are now handled by the parser instead of by luck, and an upstream HTML change degrades to "no results" exactly as before instead of mis-parsing. The `uddg=` redirect unwrap moved into the parser (`_unwrap_ddg_url`), normalization (title fallback, snippet cap, acceptance rule) is shared by both endpoints in `_collect_ddg_results`, and the lite→html fallback ladder is unchanged. One non-obvious landmine documented in-code: the parser's state attributes are deliberately `_ddg_`-prefixed because at least one optimized CPython build's `html.parser` keeps a buffered-chunk list in `self._pending` and `.clear()`s it in base `close()` — a plain `_pending` attribute would be silently emptied on patched interpreters. No caching (that stays PERF-07, ⊘ WONTFIX — live results are the feature).

### Tests

- **Tests**: 53 new tests in `tests/test_r07_15_maint_batch.py` — MAINT-07: the detect/get sweep across every `_DETECT_FAMILIES` entry, the `qwen2.5-coder` agreement pin, alias-map and longest-first partial-match pins, and the `_DEFAULT_THROUGH_FAMILIES` stability pin; MAINT-08: `StreamAccumulator` delta merging (split tool_call chunks, out-of-order indices, malformed-JSON `_raw_arguments` fallback, reasoning promotion on empty content) and `StreamRenderer` byte-exact output captured via `out=io.StringIO()` (prefix-once, panel header, indent, transition newline, cancelled shape); MAINT-10: strict-match unwrapping, injection attempts that must NOT steer the router, single re-prompt then keyword fallback, and the substring-name (`coder` vs `coder2`) regression; MAINT-15: two handles/one lock per path, realpath normalization, distinct paths stay independent, and weakref cleanup after GC; MAINT-19: cross-instance cache sharing per backend, subclass isolation, and the instance-level failure-fallback non-poisoning pin; PERF-03: parser fixtures for both endpoint layouts (attribute reordering, entities, nested tags), redirect unwrap, snippet cap, acceptance rule, and a regression pin for the `_ddg_` attribute namespacing. Suite: 1935 → **1988 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+53), zero regressions.

### Audit

- **Register roll-up**: this release closed 5 Maintainability findings + 1 Performance finding. Register: 105 findings — **40 → 34 OPEN / 58 → 64 CLOSED / 7 WONTFIX (71 archived, 68%)**. Severity (OPEN): 0 High / 17 → 15 Medium / 23 → 19 Low. Maintainability is now down to 4 OPEN findings (MAINT-01, MAINT-03, MAINT-22, MAINT-23 — all Medium), Performance is now 0 OPEN. Audit-dash + panel data regenerated from the sources (split-audit.py + generate_audit_dash.py + refresh script).

### Intra-release amendments

- **2026-09-29 — `openrouter/free` (the named Free Models Router) was missing from `agentkthx models --backend openrouter` under `OPENROUTER_FREE_ONLY=true`.** User-reported during VM smoke testing: the listing showed the 16 `:free`-suffix models but not `openrouter/free` — the plugin's DEFAULT model (`OPENROUTER_DEFAULT_MODEL`) — and the root cause was two layers deep. Layer 1 (deterministic): `cmd_models` re-filtered at the CLI level with a bare `m["name"].endswith(":free")` check, which a model literally named `openrouter/free` fails — even though the backend's own R07.09 filter had correctly accepted it via `_is_free_model()` all along; the adjacent ZAI branch already used the helper-based filter, the OpenRouter branch just never got the same treatment. Layer 2 (latent): the `/v1/models` endpoint does not reliably include the router (public listings show it today; the R07.10 note about router models returning null fields in this same endpoint shows its shape has always been flaky), so if the API omits it the backend list lacks it too. Fixes: the CLI filter now routes through the shared `_is_free_model()` helper (mirroring ZAI), and `list_models()` injects a synthetic `_free_router_entry()` whenever the API response omits the router — in both the live path and the catalog-fallback path, where FREE_ONLY previously yielded an EMPTY list since the static catalog contains no free models at all. The injected entry mirrors the live API (verified 2026-09-29: context 200000 → renders as 195K, $0/$0 pricing, `max_completion_tokens` null → 4096 per R07.10) and is dedup-guarded, so a response that DOES include the router still lists it exactly once. 10 new tests in `tests/test_r07_15_openrouter_free_listing.py` (injection, no-duplicate, FREE_ONLY survival, both fallback paths, context resolution through the MAINT-19 class cache, CLI filter unit + end-to-end `cmd_models` render) — plus one test-infra lesson worth keeping: the backend fixture must POP the init-time fallback's instance-level `_model_cache` instead of assigning None, because `instance._model_cache = None` creates a shadow that permanently blinds every later `self._model_cache` read (exactly the trap MAINT-19 documented, re-created by the test's own hands). Suite: 1988 → **1998 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+10). Pushed as `4e1555e`.
- **2026-09-29 — agent mode's verbose footer always reported "0 tool calls"** (spotted in the same VM smoke pass: the transcript showed `[1] tool shell` + `[2] tool shell` directly above a footer reading "3 steps, 0 tool calls"). `agent_mode.py` compared `step.type` against the STRING `"tool_call"` — but `StepResult.type` is a `StepResultType` ENUM member, so the comparison was always False (the count has never worked); it also collected names via a nonexistent `tool_name` attribute instead of `step.tool_call.name`. Extracted a `_step_tool_stats()` helper using the same `step.type == StepResultType.TOOL_CALL` + `step.tool_call.name` pattern as `cli/utils.py`'s step summary, with first-seen-order dedup — the "🔧 Tools used:" line now actually appears too. 6 new tests in `tests/test_r07_15_agent_mode_tool_counter.py` (counting, dedup, zero-tool runs, real-`AgentRun` acceptance, and a source pin against the string-comparison regression). Housekeeping in the same pass: audit.md header Commit synced to the real R07.15 push (`4e1555e`, was "95ab504 (R07.15 push pending)") and brief.md commit/suite lines refreshed. Suite: 1998 → **2004 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+6). Pushed as `7579e3c`.

The release and both amendments above landed as three commits all titled "R07.15" — `30cbe4f` (release), `4e1555e` (openrouter/free listing), `7579e3c` (tool-call counter) — and the push-history line under the release header plus the per-amendment hash references now make the whole chain traceable from the CHANGELOG alone. Suite unchanged: **2004 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures**. Pushed as `7f59a8d`.
- **2026-09-29 — four-finding closure batch: TEST-06, FEAT-02, ROB-11, ROB-22** (two Medium, two Low; register 34 → 30 OPEN). **TEST-06**: CI gains a non-blocking `lint` job — `ruff check` + `black --check` over `agentkthx/` and `tests/` with `continue-on-error: true` until the pre-existing drift is burned down; the stale "we don't gate on black/ruff" design note is replaced with the explicit promotion path. **FEAT-02**: per-tool `timeout` ToolParams for `http_get`/`web_search` (defaults 30/15 preserved, clamped to 1–300 s since `urlopen(timeout <= 0)` disables the socket timeout; both DuckDuckGo endpoint attempts carry it) plus concurrent execution of INDEPENDENT tool-call batches — the loop body's inline per-call loop is replaced by a batch dispatcher that gates + creates FunctionCallItems in call order (via `_gate_and_prepare_tool_call`, extracted verbatim from `_execute_single_tool_call`), executes the handlers on a `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=min(4, batch))`, and commits through the shared `_commit_tool_result` in ORIGINAL call order — memory pairing and the step timeline are byte-identical to the sequential path regardless of worker completion order. Safety rules: shell/write_file/edit_file/todo are never parallelized (filesystem/state mutations), duplicate (tool, args) pairs stay sequential (the R06.52 repeat-guard counts per pair), `AGENTKTHX_PARALLEL_TOOLS=0` is the escape hatch, and Ctrl+C keeps ROB-01 semantics (cancel futures, mark CANCELLED, ERROR step, break). The MAINT-04 dedup pins were updated to the shared-gate structure. **ROB-11**: transactional plugin registration per the audit's recommendation — a `_PluginTransaction` records an undo for every imperative `register_backend`/`register_tool`/`register_cli_command`/`register_hook` call made during `register()` and rolls them back LIFO (prev-value-restore, identity-checked, so earlier same-name registrations survive a later plugin's overwrite-and-fail) when a load fails — closing the gap where a missing/raising/incomplete plugin `unregister()` left partial registrations alive; failures firing BEFORE `register()` (import error, missing register, sha256 pin mismatch) roll back nothing. **ROB-22**: OrcaRouter's `_iter_sse_lines` gains the post-loop `OrcaRouter-Stream: exhausted retries (4 attempts)` raise matching the non-streaming path — a bounded retry generator now always ends in yield-or-raise instead of being one drift away from a silent empty stream. 27 new tests in `tests/test_r07_15_rob11_rob22_feat02_test06.py`. Suite: 2004 → **2031 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+27). Register: 105 findings — **30 OPEN / 68 CLOSED / 7 WONTFIX (75 archived, 71%)**. Pushed as `53916a5`.
- **2026-09-29 — two interactive-mode UX fixes from the four-finding-batch smoke pass** (user-reported while smoke-testing the parallel-tools work). **(1) `--backend` help text listed a stale, hardcoded subset of backends** — `chat -h`, `agent -h` and `run -h` all named "ollama, bitnet, llama-server, zai, openrouter, huggingface, gemini, openai", silently omitting the plugin backends that worked fine at runtime: `mistral`, `orcarouter`, `pollinations`, plus every alias (`hf`, `mst`, `oai`, `orca`, `poll`, `llama_server`). The list had drifted because it lived as a literal string in `shared_args.add_agent_args()` while the registry grew around it. Fix: the help string is now built at parser-build time from `get_backend_choices()` — the same merged native+plugin registry `models -h` already rendered as argparse `choices` — which works because the CLI's `main()` loads all plugins BEFORE `create_parser()`; a fallback core list keeps the help informative when `add_agent_args` is used by standalone example scripts where no plugin manager has run. **(2) Chat mode now shows the per-response stats agent mode has shown since the tool-call-counter fix**: one dim `⏱️ N steps, M tool calls, Xms` line under every completed response (plus `🔧 Tools used: …` when the turn made tool calls). The formatting lives in a shared `agent_mode._format_response_stats()` helper at the library layer — agent mode's verbose footer was refactored onto the same helper (only behavior delta: singular "1 step"/"1 tool call" instead of a plural-only format), so the two interactive modes can no longer drift apart. Chat renders the line for streaming and non-streaming turns alike, including empty/throttled responses (where the ms figure shows how long the failed turn burned), always before the persistent footer refresh. 20 new tests in `tests/test_r07_15_chat_stats_backend_help.py` (all three subcommands' rendered help, the dynamic/fallback registry paths, a no-hardcoded-list source pin, the stats format matrix incl. singular/plural + name dedup + duck-typed runs, and wiring pins for both call sites). Suite: 2031 → **2051 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+20). Pushed as `ea98951`.
- **2026-09-29 — CI was red since the four-finding batch: the TEST-06 CI-pinning tests hard-import `yaml` but `pyyaml` was not in the dev extras** (first noticed on the UX-fix push — all three pytest-bearing jobs reported `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'yaml'`). The three `TestTest06CILintJob` tests added in the four-finding batch (`53916a5`) parse `.github/workflows/ci.yml` with `yaml.safe_load` to pin the lint job's shape, but CI installs exactly `pip install -e .[dev]` and the dev extras carried only pytest/pytest-cov/black/ruff — zero runtime deps is the project's stated design, so the sandbox's PyYAML never existed in Actions. The result was the embarrassing kind of failure: the tests whose entire job is to verify CI could not run IN CI, and the previous batch's push went red unnoticed. Fix: `pyyaml>=6.0` joins `[project.optional-dependencies].dev` (one wheel, no transitive deps, pip cache key is pyproject.toml so CI busts correctly), and a comment at the import now documents the contract — these pins must RUN in CI, never `importorskip`: a skip would leave CI unable to check its own config drift. Verified by reproducing the CI environment exactly (fresh venv, `pip install -e .[dev]` only): the three failing tests recover, and both the plain pytest matrix and the coverage job (56% total) go green. Suite unchanged: **2051 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (no new tests — the 3 previously-CI-blind pins now actually execute in CI). Pushed as `8b86fb7`.
- **2026-09-29 — lint burn-down: ~2.8k pre-tooling drift findings → zero, and the lint job promoted from non-blocking to a REQUIRED check.** The `lint (ruff + black)` job added in the four-finding batch had been red since its very first run — the ci.yml comment predicted as much ("a gating switch would fail on the first run") — but it stayed the last red thing after the pyyaml fix turned the pytest matrix and coverage green. Burn-down in three passes: (1) `ruff check --fix` resolved 1,886 mechanical findings (import sorting I001, whitespace W291/292/293, empty f-strings F541, unused imports F401); (2) `black` reformatted 178 files to the shared 100-col style — which also eliminated the W293 stragglers ruff's fixer treats as unsafe inside multi-line strings and folded most E701 compound one-liners; (3) 45 hand fixes (exact-match-asserted script) for the judgment classes: F841 dead locals deleted where trivially pure (`buffer`, `state_out`, `start_time`, dead dict.get chains) or converted to bare-expression calls where the RHS has observable effects (network calls in ACP/examples, env-mutating backend constructors in tests, `host._execute_tool_calls` state mutation); E741/E731/E721 modernized (`l` → `ldr`, lambdas → defs, `annotation == bool` → `annotation is bool`); one F811 (a nested `class tools:` shadowed by a same-name property); per-file E402 suppressions documented in pyproject for the four setup-then-import patterns (package `__init__` version resolution, ACP config imports, skill scripts' sys.path/sys.modules manipulation, tests inserting REPO_ROOT before backend imports). The exercise paid for itself with THREE latent bugs the linters caught: `huggingface.HF_MODELS` carried a copy-pasted dict KEY — `"prism-ml/Ternary-Bonsai-27B-gguf"` holding gpt-oss-20b's metadata (provider "openai", the wrong description) — which silently shadowed BOTH the real Ternary-Bonsai entry AND the `openai/gpt-oss-20b` catalog entry the block was written to define (key renamed, entry restored); gemini's rate-limit table defined `gemini-2.5-pro-preview-tts` twice (identical values, duplicate removed); and `soul/types.py` annotated `SoulManifest.validate(soul_dir: Optional["Path"])` without importing `Path` — a forward reference that explodes under `typing.get_type_hints()`. Two retry loops' dead `last_retryable_error` bookkeeping (assigned, never read, referenced by no test) was removed in gemini/openrouter. Policy: E501 moved to `lint.ignore` with rationale in pyproject — black owns line length, and the ~310 remaining hits are unbreakable string literals (SSE fixtures, URLs, prompt templates) black itself accepts. ## [R07.14] - 2026-09-29 1:45:36 AM

**Performance quick-wins + smoke-test hotfix release — five findings closed (PERF-01, PERF-02, PERF-04, PERF-06, ROB-32) plus one register correction (PERF-03's recommendation), 35 new tests, zero regressions.** The perf batch eliminates the three per-step hot spots in the agentic loop's memory/compaction path, makes forced plugin rescans mtime-aware, and bounds the update check's PyPI read. The smoke-test hotfix (ROB-32) fixes a crash on EVERY `agentkthx chat`/`agentkthx agent` invocation: `_build_agent` still passed the `force_react` kwarg whose `Agent.__init__` parameter silently vanished in R03.3, and ARCH-05's fail-fast (R07.13) turned that latent drift into a hard TypeError — the flag is now re-promoted (parameter #30) and wired into `ToolParser` as ReAct-only parsing, its documented meaning. PERF-03 (`web_search` DuckDuckGo parsing) stays OPEN — see the correction below. Suite: 1882 → **1935 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+18 tests in `tests/test_r07_13_perf_quick_wins.py`, +18 tests in `tests/test_r07_14_perf_quick_wins.py`, +17 ROB-32 tests in `tests/test_r07_14_force_react.py`).

### Performance

- **PERF-01 CLOSED — `Memory.get_messages()` sanitizes only after mutation** (`core/memory.py`): the R06.52 `sanitize_history()` repair (two passes: orphan drop + placeholder fill, O(n × tool_calls)) used to run at the top of EVERY `get_messages()` — once per agentic step even when nothing had changed. A `_sanitize_dirty` flag now gates it: every mutating path invalidates (`add()` — the funnel for `add_tool_call`/`add_tool_result` — plus `clear()`, `compact_messages()`, and `PersistentMemory.load()`), `get_messages()` sanitizes only when dirty, and `sanitize_history()` marks the state clean at its end (still idempotent and directly callable). On the audit's 25-step/50-message example: 25 repair runs per agent run → one per mutation. Zero behavioral change — the pairing guarantees are pinned by the existing suite plus 4 new invalidation tests.
- **PERF-02 CLOSED — compaction heuristic size estimate cached on Memory** (`core/memory.py` + `core/compaction.py`): `_check_compaction`, `_snapshot_running_tokens`, and `_update_running_tokens` each re-scanned the full history with a `json.dumps` per assistant tool_calls message — 3 scans + up to 2×n dumps per agentic step. New `Memory.estimated_chars()` caches the exact same quantity, invalidated by the same funnel as PERF-01 and recomputed lazily; a single `_estimate_memory_chars()` helper routes all three consumers through it (zero `json.dumps` on a warm cache — pinned by test). The estimate formula is unchanged, so compaction thresholds behave identically. The helper falls back to the historical inline scan for non-`Memory` memories via a mock-safe `isinstance(memory, Memory)` predicate (a duck-type check broke test doubles — MagicMock auto-attributes are callable), keeping the documented host contract intact.
- **PERF-04 CLOSED — `PluginManager.discover(force=True)` mtime-aware** (`plugins/_loader.py`): a forced rescan used to re-parse every `plugin.json` in every root. A `_manifest_cache: dict[path, (st_mtime, manifest)]` now reuses unchanged manifests BY IDENTITY and re-parses only new/modified files; the cache is pruned to paths seen in the latest scan so removed plugins drop from both results and cache (a re-created path with a stale-equal mtime re-parses — pinned by test). Dedup/first-root-wins semantics untouched. Documented caveat (accepted): a file touched twice within one mtime tick can slip through — dev-reload nicety, not a correctness boundary.
- **PERF-06 CLOSED — `update_check._fetch_json` bounded bytes-direct read** (`update_check.py`): `resp.read().decode("utf-8")` held an unbounded bytes+str pair of the PyPI document (live-measured 2026-09-29: 94,189 bytes — the full README embeds as `info.description`). Now `_MAX_UPDATE_JSON_BYTES = 262144` and `json.loads(resp.read(cap))` parses directly from bytes. Both original recommendations were rejected on evidence: `json.load(resp)` does NOT stream (it calls `fp.read()` internally), and the "first 4KB contains `info.version`" idea breaks on PyPI's alphabetical key order (`description` precedes `version`). The cap is ~2.7× headroom: well-formed bodies parse byte-identically, an over-cap body raises `JSONDecodeError` which `check_for_update` already swallows per-source (best-effort, retried next invocation). `test_update_check`'s `_FakeResponse.read` widened to urllib's real `read(amt)` contract.
- **PERF-03 recommendation CORRECTED (finding stays OPEN)**: the register's original suggestion — "use a JSON API (DuckDuckGo has `https://api.duckduckgo.com/?q=...&format=json`)" — is invalid, per user confirmation: that endpoint is the Instant Answer API (topic snapshots only, no web search results), and DuckDuckGo publishes no JSON output for web search at all; `lite`/`html.duckduckgo.com` are HTML-only. The corrected recommendation is a stdlib `html.parser` rewrite (attribute-order/whitespace-resilient where the 4 regexes are not) with the lite→html fallback ladder kept; result caching remains PERF-07 (⊘ WONTFIX — intentional).

### Robustness

- **ROB-32 CLOSED — `force_react` re-promoted and wired to ReAct-only parsing; every `agentkthx chat`/`agentkthx agent` invocation crashed post-ARCH-05** (`cli/agent_factory.py`, `core/agent_setup.py`, `core/tool_parse.py`): a user smoke test of the R07.14 build hit `TypeError: Agent.__init__ got unexpected keyword argument(s): force_react` on the very first command. `_build_agent` has passed `force_react=args.force_react` since the R07.00 CLI split, but the attribute silently vanished from `Agent.__init__` in R03.3 — the kwarg was swallowed by `**kwargs` and did nothing for the better part of a year of releases, until ARCH-05 (R07.13) made unknown kwargs fail fast and turned the dormant drift into a hard crash on both CLI entry points (the factory passes the flag unconditionally, so the value was irrelevant — the kwarg name itself was rejected). The suite couldn't catch it because the CLI tests patch `_build_agent` itself — the factory-to-Agent contract was never exercised end-to-end. The fix re-promotes the flag rather than dropping it: it is real, user-facing surface (README documents `Agent(model=..., force_react=True)`; `shared_args` exposes `--force-react` and `AGENTKTHX_FORCE_REACT=1`). `force_react: bool = False` is now parameter #30 on `AgentSetupMixin.__init__` (stored, listed in the ARCH-05 valid-kwargs message) and is threaded into `ToolParser` at both construction sites (initial + the `register_tool` rebuild, matching ROB-19's debug-flag reasoning). `ToolParser(force_react=True)` enforces ReAct-only parsing — the flag's documented meaning ("Force ReAct mode for tool calling"): only explicit `Action:`/`Action Input:` blocks produce tool calls; the native-JSON and XML matchers are skipped since on models that emit ReAct they add no recall and can only misfire. Default False keeps the standing native → ReAct → XML chain byte-identical. The systemic gap is pinned by 17 new tests in `tests/test_r07_14_force_react.py`, including one that drives a REAL Agent through `_build_agent` (backend stubbed, factory unpatched) and one that asserts the full captured-kwarg set against `inspect.signature(Agent.__init__)` — future factory/signature drift fails in the suite, not in a user's terminal.

### Tests

- **Tests**: 18 new tests in `tests/test_r07_14_perf_quick_wins.py` (4 PERF-01 sanitize-cache invalidation, 6 PERF-02 estimate-cache + mixin routing incl. a zero-rescan pin and the foreign-memory fallback, 4 PERF-04 mtime reuse/reparse/removal, 4 PERF-06 bounded read + silent-failure path). `tests/test_update_check.py`'s `_FakeResponse` updated for the `read(amt)` contract. Plus 17 ROB-32 tests in `tests/test_r07_14_force_react.py` (Agent param default/storage/signature, ToolParser ReAct-only enforcement incl. skip pins for the native-JSON and XML shapes, `register_tool` rebuild threading, the `_build_agent` real-Agent contract, and the `AGENTKTHX_FORCE_REACT` env path). Suite: 1882 → **1935 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+18 R07.13 perf-pin tests in `tests/test_r07_13_perf_quick_wins.py` rode along with this push — see Intra-release amendments).

### Audit

- **Register roll-up**: this release closed 4 PERF findings + 1 High Robustness finding (ROB-32 — opened by the user smoke test and closed in the same release) and corrected one recommendation in place (PERF-03 — stays OPEN; DuckDuckGo publishes no web-search JSON output, so stdlib HTML parsing remains the only path; result caching stays PERF-07, ⊘ WONTFIX). Register: 105 findings — **44 → 40 OPEN / 57 → 58 CLOSED / 7 WONTFIX (65 archived, 62%)**. Severity (OPEN): 0 High / 17 Medium / 23 Low. Audit-dash + panel data regenerated from the sources (split-audit.py + generate_audit_dash.py + refresh script).

### Intra-release amendments

- **2026-09-29 — suite line corrected: 1917 → 1935.** The R07.14 push carried `tests/test_r07_13_perf_quick_wins.py` — 18 tests written as R07.13 intra-release perf pins after the R07.13 push, so that release's 1882 baseline never counted them — alongside this release's own 35. Both suite lines above now read 1882 → **1935 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures**: 1882 (R07.13) + 18 (`test_r07_13_perf_quick_wins.py`) + 18 (`test_r07_14_perf_quick_wins.py`) + 17 (`test_r07_14_force_react.py`) = 1935. Verified live post-push: `python -m pytest -q` → 1935 passed / 16 skipped / 0 failures in ~28s. Standing rule: when a push carries test files from earlier intra-release work, the CHANGELOG suite line moves IN THAT PUSH — a stale line silently feeds the panel/audit data until a live `--pytest` run exposes the drift.

## [R07.13] - 2026-09-28 8:21:49 PM

**Probe suite expansion + static catalog cleanup + ARCH closure batch — all 10 backend probe scripts expanded to a uniform 6-section report format (4,773 lines total, verified live against 7 cloud APIs / 841 live models); 11 retired catalog entries removed (9 OpenRouter + 2 OpenAI) after live API drift confirmed they're no longer served; all 5 OPEN Architecture findings CLOSED in a single pass (ARCH-02, ARCH-03, ARCH-04, ARCH-05, ARCH-06) — Architecture category now 0 OPEN.** The probe expansion delivers the 11 fields the user requested (full model catalog, max context, max tokens response, model size available, pricing available, is_free, is_paid, request params, response params, tool calling format, free vs full catalog) across every backend, with per-backend free-tier rules mirrored verbatim from the backend source code (not invented). Local backends (ollama, llama_server, bitnet) mark pricing/free/paid as N/A and substitute "installed vs running models" for Section 6. The catalog cleanup retired 9 OpenRouter entries confirmed absent from the live `/models` endpoint (all 4 Anthropic Claude 3.x variants, both Cohere Command-R models, both Google Gemini 1.5 models, and Mistral Mixtral 8x7B — all superseded by newer generations) and 2 OpenAI entries past their `shutdown_date` (gpt-5-codex and gpt-5.1-codex, both with shutdown_date 2026-07-23, 68 days past at cleanup time). The ARCH closure batch closed every OPEN Architecture finding: `SSEEventBuilder` extraction (ARCH-02, 163→57-line generator), AgentMode OpenResponses event integration (ARCH-03, opt-in `event_emitter` callback), soul loader path resolution consolidation (ARCH-04, 5-step chain → linear 6-step), kwargs swallowing closure (ARCH-05, 5 stashed kwargs promoted to explicit named params, typos now raise `TypeError`), and `WireAdapter` protocol for non-OpenAI cloud wire shapes (ARCH-06, documents the seam for a future Anthropic Messages API backend). Suite: 1849 → **1882 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+33 tests in `tests/test_r07_13_arch_closures.py`).

### Architecture

- **ARCH-02 CLOSED — `SSEEventBuilder` extracted from the 163-line `stream_response_events` generator** (`core/openresponses.py`): the generator now delegates to a new `SSEEventBuilder` class with 10 `emit_*` methods (9 event types + error path). Each method handles the protocol details AND the response state mutation for one event type (`emit_queued()`, `emit_in_progress()`, `emit_output_item_added()`, `emit_content_part_added()`, `emit_delta(chunk)`, `emit_text_done()`, `emit_content_part_done()`, `emit_output_item_done()`, `emit_completed()`, `emit_failed(error)`). The generator is now a 57-line thin orchestration loop over the builder (was 163 lines). The builder is testable in isolation — each event transition can be unit-tested without running the full generator. External contract preserved: same SSE event sequence, same response state mutations, same error-path handling.

- **ARCH-03 CLOSED — `AgentMode` integrated with the OpenResponses event stream** (`agent_mode.py`): added an opt-in `event_emitter` callback. When set, `AgentMode.run_task()` emits 6 OpenResponses event types: `response.created` (with goal + plan), `response.output_item.added` (per step), `response.output_item.done` (×2 for success/fail, with step + success + msg), `response.completed` (with goal + final_response), `response.failed` (with goal + error + step). The `TaskPlan`/`Step`/`Action` dataclasses remain unchanged — they map onto `Item` events at emission time. When `event_emitter` is None (default), behavior is unchanged — the `agent` subcommand doesn't set this, preserving existing behavior. Library callers who want the event stream pass `event_emitter=my_callback`. Broken emitters are swallowed (never crash the task loop). Deprecation was ruled out: the `agentkthx agent` subcommand is documented and actively uses `AgentMode`. **Verified end-to-end against `openrouter/free`** — 4 events emitted in 3.4s (created → output_item.added → output_item.done → completed), zero credits burned.

- **ARCH-04 CLOSED — soul loader path resolution consolidated** (`soul/loader.py`): the 5-step chain with repeated `try/except (ImportError, TypeError, AttributeError)` blocks is now a single linear 6-step algorithm with documented search order: (1) absolute path, (2) relative to CWD, (3) `importlib.resources.files('agentkthx')` (canonical accessor — works for namespace packages, Windows pip installs, editable installs), (4) filesystem fallback `<package_dir>/souls/<path>` (covers zipped installs + Python versions where `importlib.resources.files` returns a traversable that doesn't exist on disk), (5) bare soul name (no path separators — try `souls/<name>` then `souls/<name>/soul.json`), (6) original path as last-resort (covers symlink / PATH resolution). Single try/except per fallback — no nesting, no fallback-within-fallback. Behavior preserved exactly: `nova-helper` default soul still loads, all resolution paths return Path or None (never raises).

- **ARCH-05 CLOSED — kwargs swallowing pattern closed** (`core/agent_setup.py`): the 5 stashed kwargs (`response_format`, `confirm_dangerous`, `persistent`, `session_id`, `memory_db`) are now explicit named parameters on `AgentSetupMixin.__init__`. The `**kwargs` pattern is retained but now fails fast: any unknown kwarg raises `TypeError` with a message listing all 29 valid parameters. Typos (e.g. `persistant=True` instead of `persistent=True`) now surface immediately instead of being silently swallowed. Backward compat preserved: all existing call sites pass these as kwargs, which still work. The `session_id` parameter, when provided, overrides the generated UUID (preserving the historical behavior where the stashed session_id took precedence for persistent memory restoration).

- **ARCH-06 CLOSED — `WireAdapter` protocol for non-OpenAI cloud wire shapes** (`backends/cloud_base.py`): introduced a new `WireAdapter` class documenting the seam where non-OpenAI clouds (e.g. Anthropic Messages API) plug in a custom wire format without forking `OpenAICompatibleBackend`. The protocol has 3 hook methods: `build_request_body()` (translate messages → provider-native body), `parse_response()` (translate provider response → AgentKthx shape), `iter_sse_events()` (translate provider SSE events → AgentKthx chunks). All 3 return `None` by default → fall back to the inherited OpenAI-shape implementation. `CloudBackend._wire_adapter` defaults to `None` (OpenAI shape, backward compat). All 4 existing cloud backends (ZAI, Mistral, OpenRouter, Pollinations) inherit `None` — no behavior change. A future Anthropic Messages API backend would subclass `CloudBackend`, set `_wire_adapter = AnthropicWireAdapter()`, and override the 3 hooks to translate between OpenAI-shape messages and Anthropic's `system` + `messages` split + `content_block_*` SSE events. The coupling is now explicit and documented rather than implicit.

### Probes

- **All 10 probe scripts expanded to 6-section format** (`scripts/probe_*.sh`): each probe now produces a uniform report with (1) Endpoint, (2) Static API surface, (3) Live /models response, (4) Card field availability matrix, (5) Per-model detail table, (6) Free vs full catalog. Cloud backends (7: openai, gemini, mistral, zai, openrouter, huggingface, pollinations) get the full 11-field treatment including pricing and free/paid classification. Local backends (3: ollama, llama_server, bitnet) mark pricing/free/paid as N/A and substitute "installed vs running models" for Section 6. Total: 4,773 lines across 10 files (was ~650 lines combined before expansion). Each probe is standalone (bash + python3 + curl, no shared module) and mirrors its backend's actual free-tier rule from the plugin source code.

## [R07.12] - 2026-09-28 3:15:17 PM

**Audit closure release — the first release dedicated to driving the audit register down: 5 findings CLOSED, 2 WONTFIX, +23 regression tests, zero new findings.** Closes the SEC-11 cluster (SEC-11 + SEC-17 + ROB-27 — the "single coordinated fix" the R07.08 next-release priorities prescribed): DNS resolution in the SSRF validator is now bounded end-to-end, and the redirect chain carries an explicit hop budget. OrcaRouter free-model detection becomes live-convention-driven (ROB-23), and ZAI's unknown-model placeholder becomes honest (ROB-24). SEC-18 and SEC-19 (provider-controlled error prose in `RuntimeError` messages) move to WONTFIX by owner decision. Register: 104 findings — **55 OPEN / 42 CLOSED / 7 WONTFIX (49 archived, 47%)**, was 62 open at R07.11 (release-tag snapshot — the intra-release quick-wins batch below closes six more, so the final R07.12 register is **49 OPEN / 48 CLOSED / 7 WONTFIX, 55 archived, 53%**, which is what the audit-dash serves). Suite 1751 → **1774 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+23 tests in `tests/test_r07_12_closure_batch.py`).

### Security

- **SEC-11 CLOSED — bounded DNS resolution in the SSRF validator** (`core/helpers.py`): `socket.getaddrinfo` is a synchronous C call with no timeout parameter; called directly it let a malicious or broken resolver stall the agent indefinitely, and a hostname resolving to thousands of A records amplified the cost. New `_resolve_hostname_bounded()` runs the resolution on a daemon thread joined with `_DNS_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 5.0`; `_iter_hostname_ips` caps examined records at `_MAX_DNS_RECORDS = 32` and returns the `__DNS_TIMEOUT__` sentinel on timeout, which `is_safe_url` fails CLOSED on ("DNS resolution of '<host>' timed out after 5s (possible DoS)") — skipping the SSRF check is exactly when DNS manipulation pays off. Genuine resolution failures (NXDOMAIN) keep the historical fail-open contract; IP literals still short-circuit before the DNS path entirely. The per-host DNS cache from the R07.08 prescription was deliberately omitted: with resolution bounded and thread-isolated, repeated lookups are a performance nit, not a DoS.
- **SEC-17 CLOSED — explicit redirect-hop budget** (`tools/builtins.py`): `_SSRFSafeRedirectHandler` now tracks `_hops_followed` per handler instance and refuses hop 6+ with `URLError("redirect chain exceeded 5 hops (possible redirect DoS)")`. urllib's own `max_redirections = 10` remains as the outer backstop; the tighter explicit cap exists because each hop triggers a full SSRF validation including a DNS resolution — bounded to ~5s each since this release, so 5 hops keeps attacker-controlled validator cost (~25s) inside the realm of the 30s HTTP request timeout instead of 10 × unbounded. SEC-03's per-hop re-validation of every redirect target is unchanged.
- **SEC-18 + SEC-19 WONTFIX (owner decision) — provider-controlled error prose in `RuntimeError` messages** (Mistral `mistral.py`, Pollinations `pollinations.py`): first-party API providers are trusted parties (users hand them payment credentials at signup); the response channel strictly dominates the error channel — every turn the model consumes provider-generated text as the conversation itself, so sanitizing error prose while trusting response prose locks the window while the front door stands open; backend error prose propagates out of `generate()` → retry exhaustion → the CLI → the human terminal and never re-enters model context (the tool-output path that does reach the model is wrapped by the R07.04 SEC-10/FEAT-01 sanitization); and the single path where provider text was machine-parsed for control-flow decisions was SEC-14, closed in R07.08. The recommended `sanitize_provider_message()` consolidation is retired; the community-router aggravation on SEC-19 is response-channel-dominated (the opt-in exception — fallback onto out-of-entitlement community models — remains tracked as ROB-31).

### Robustness

- **ROB-27 CLOSED — DNS lookups no longer stall outside the request timeout**: the `timeout=30` on `opener.open()` never covered the `getaddrinfo` calls made inside `redirect_request`. Same cluster fix as SEC-11: resolution runs under the 5s wall-clock budget, timed-out lookups fail closed through the sentinel, and the abandoned daemon thread dies with the OS resolver timeout without blocking interpreter exit. Worst-case validator cost per hop ~5s; with the hop budget the whole chain is bounded at ~25s.
- **ROB-23 CLOSED — OrcaRouter free-model detection is live-convention-driven** (`plugins/orcarouter/orcarouter.py`): `_is_free_model()` now honors the upstream `-free` suffix convention (case-insensitive) — every documented OrcaRouter free model follows it — so new free models appear under `ORCAROUTER_FREE_ONLY` from the live `/v1/models` feed with no code update. The static `ORCAROUTER_FREE_MODEL_WHITELIST` is retained as the belt-and-braces floor for the outage-fallback path (where no live feed exists) and for future IDs that break the convention. `orcarouter/free` stays always-free; `orcarouter/auto` stays paid.
- **ROB-24 CLOSED — ZAI unknown-model placeholders are honest** (`plugins/zai/zai.py`): `get_model_info()` for models outside the static catalog now marks the entry `details["catalog_status"] = "unknown"` (callers can distinguish fabricated entries from catalog hits), fires an `AGENTKTHX_DEBUG` warning ("Model 'X' not in static catalog — using placeholder entry (context_length=128000, free_tier=False)"), and appends a stdlib-`difflib` "did you mean" close-match hint for likely typos (cutoff 0.8, best-effort, never blocks the lookup). Context stays at the 128K `_DEFAULT_CONTEXT_FALLBACK` deliberately: ZAI accepts IDs newer than the static catalog (the documented MAINT-02 behavior), over-reporting context self-corrects via the ARCH-03 context-length-400 recovery, and under-reporting would over-compact needlessly.

  - `TestBoundedDnsResolution` (8) — timeout returns None fast (1s-sleeping fake resolver, elapsed < 0.9s), success returns the address list, `gaierror` maps to empty (fail-open contract), record cap truncates at 32, the `__DNS_TIMEOUT__` sentinel fails `is_safe_url` CLOSED, unresolvable stays fail-open, IP literals never touch DNS, timeout/record constants pinned to sane ranges.
  - `TestRedirectHopCap` (4) — five hops allowed then the sixth rejected, the budget resets per handler instance (per request), an unsafe target is still blocked on the first hop (SEC-03 regression), `_MAX_HOPS` stays strictly below urllib's 10.

### Intra-release amendments (same day, no version bump)

- **ZAI insufficient-credits now performs the proper session model switch via the `/model` path** (`plugins/zai/zai.py`, `cli/agent_factory.py`): the historical recovery was per-request — every request re-attempted the paid model, ate the 429, and silently served that one response from the free fallback while `agent.model` (footer) and every per-model derived value (`num_ctx`, `num_predict`, family config) still described the paid model; an interim internal fallback-flag map had the same blind spot. Now the backend only *reports* the event: both 429 handlers (streaming `_iter_sse_lines` + non-streaming `_generate_with_auth`) still retry the CURRENT request on `ZAI_FREE_FALLBACK_MODEL` inline so the turn completes, then fire `set_model_switch_callback(failed_model, fallback_model)` — registered centrally in `_build_agent` via `register_insufficient_credits_switch()` — whose handler runs the exact same code path as the in-chat `/model` command (`apply_model_switch`): `num_ctx` / `num_predict` re-derived from the new model's catalog (unpinned values only), model family config refreshed, stale `_context_safe_max_tokens` cleared, and the footer reflects the new model. Guards: the switch fires only while `agent.model` is still the failed model (an explicit mid-flight `/model` by the user is never clobbered); without a registered callback (library / one-shot usage) the per-request fallback remains; a broken callback is swallowed (debug-visible) and never crashes the turn. **12 new tests in `tests/test_zai_session_fallback.py`** — callback fired with the paid id on both stream paths, per-request fallback intact without a callback, broken-callback resilience, free-model 429 never fires, switch-path integration (num_ctx 204800→132000, num_predict None→98304, safe-max-tokens cleared, notice printed), user-switch guard, hook-less backends skipped.

## [R07.11] - 2026-09-28 1:22:41 AM

**Pollinations unified-gateway plugin added — 12th bundled plugin, 7th cloud backend, 11th backend type — with two live-gateway behaviors that are NOT in the technical reference, both caught by user VM testing and fixed intra-release (v0.1.0 scaffold → v0.1.1 → v0.1.2).** Adds the `pollinations` plugin: an OpenAI-compatible aggregator surface at `https://gen.pollinations.ai/v1` where one key (or no key at all) spans `openai/`, `anthropic/`, `google/`, `z-ai/`, `deepseek/`, `qwen/`, `mistralai/`, `meta/`, `inclusionai/`, `community/` vendors and more, plus image/video/TTS/embedding cards the catalog surfaces. It is the ONLY backend that runs on the anonymous tier — a keyless-tolerant `__init__` bypasses `CloudBackend`'s hard key requirement (catalog GET and generation both work without a key; `sk_`/`pk_` prefixes validated when a key IS set). Resolution normalizes provider/model ids through a three-layer alias map (static seeds + live `aliases[]` + catalog short-names), and unknown models pass through to the gateway with a safe default card (Pollinations accepts any valid catalog id — verified live with a community `:free` variant). The catalog is live-card-first (13-model static fallback), listings surface `health {status, success_rate, requests}` telemetry, and `healthy_fallbacks()` orders by `success_rate` desc / price asc. Error handling covers the Pollinations error envelope with `requestId` surfaced, 402 pollen-budget exhaustion mapped non-retryable, `Retry-After` honored but capped at 60s (the ROB-16 pattern), and ARCH-03 context-400 recovery. Ships with the `poll` CLI alias, 8 `POLLINATIONS_*` env vars, `BackendType.POLLINATIONS`, a GET-only zero-pollen probe script, and 94 tests (91 unit + 3 live-gated). Suite 1660 → **1751 passed, 16 skipped, 0 failures**.

Two live-gateway discoveries drove the two intra-release revisions (c18bdff, 3a9bb6f):

1. **Entitlement scoping on `GET /v1/models`** — the gateway scopes the catalog to the key's entitlements when a Bearer key is sent: 307 cards anonymous vs 134 keyed with the test key, directly contradicting the reference doc's "bearer key returns the same data" claim (line 92 of `docs/api/POLLINATIONS_API_TECHNICAL_REFERENCE.md`). Cross-referencing the bare `GET /models` feed proves the boundary: the 134 keyed cards are EXACTLY the `paid_only != True` subset (the free TIER — Quest-Pollen-eligible models, still metered pollen per token but runnable on the free grant); the 173 `paid_only: true` models are invisible to the keyed feed. The `paid_only` field itself only exists on the bare `/models` feed — it is absent from `/v1/models` cards entirely. → v0.1.1 added `POLLINATIONS_ANON_CATALOG`: catalog GETs go anonymous (full 307-card public view) while generation POSTs stay keyed.

2. **Zero-cost pricing encoding** — TRUE zero-cost models (the `:free`/`-free` community variants — 16 text cards live at release time) are encoded as currency-only pricing dicts `{"currency": "pollen"}` with NO price fields at all; price fields are never zero-valued on the live feed. The scaffold's `_card_is_free()` predicate required zero-valued price fields and could therefore never match on ANY live feed, anonymous or keyed — `FREE_ONLY` listed 0 models everywhere while the enter.pollinations.ai website showed 30+. → v0.1.2 fixed the predicate: pricing keys ⊆ `{currency}` → free (zero-valued and static `input`/`output == 0` branches kept; partial-zero stays conservative not-free). With the predicate fixed, the keyed entitlement feed turns out to carry all 16 zero-cost cards — **`FREE_ONLY` now works in every key state and no longer requires `ANON_CATALOG`**, which is demoted to a pure browse-breadth toggle (~190-card public view vs 109-card entitlement view).

v0.1.1 also fixed the FREE_ONLY generate-time redirect: it previously landed on `POLLINATIONS_FALLBACK_MODEL` (`z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`), which is itself priced — pollen burned either way. The redirect now prefers `_first_free_model()` (healthiest zero-cost text card: `success_rate` desc, id asc tiebreak, non-text categories excluded) and only degrades to the configured fallback, with an `AGENTKTHX_DEBUG` warning, when no zero-cost card exists at all.

Validated end-to-end on the user's VM with a real key: **190** models listed with key + `ANON_CATALOG=1` / **109** keyed (102 entitlement text cards + the 7 static seeds the keyed feed drops) / **189** keyless / **16** with `FREE_ONLY=1` in all three key states. Live chat with `--stream --think` on `community/vendouple/muse-glimmer-30b:free` (a model in NO directory listing) returned reasoning blocks and usage stats correctly — pass-through normalization works. (The 190-vs-189 keyless delta is live catalog churn: community models are added/removed in real time — the card count was observed oscillating 307↔308 during the release session, and one free model's publisher path changed between observations.)

### Features

- **`pollinations` plugin (new, 12th bundled plugin / 7th cloud backend / 11th `BackendType`)** — `agentkthx/plugins/pollinations/` ships `plugin.json` (v0.2 manifest, `poll` CLI alias), `__init__.py` (`register()` / `unregister()`, `poll` registered `alias_of="pollinations"`), and `pollinations.py` (1,744 lines): `PollinationsBackend(CloudBackend)` + `POLLINATIONS_MODELS` 13-entry static catalog (`anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`, `anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6`, `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash`, `google/gemini-3.7-flash`, `meta/llama-4-scout`, …) + `_fetch_model_cards()` + `_card_is_free()` + `_first_free_model()`. Joins ZAI, OpenRouter, Gemini, OpenAI, HuggingFace, and Mistral as the 7th cloud backend; `BackendType.POLLINATIONS` joins the enum as the 11th backend type.

- **Wire-format surface** — built per `docs/api/POLLINATIONS_API_TECHNICAL_REFERENCE.md` (Sep-27 snapshot, 311 cards): `seed` passthrough (no `random_seed` aliasing — opposite of Mistral), `reasoning_effort` pass-through, `POLLINATIONS_SAFE` safe-flag injection (read live from env), usage-null normalization for media models (their `/v1/chat/completions` omit usage), SSE streaming via the inherited `generate_completions_stream()` + usage-strictness debug warning, `get_balance()` pollen probe, and unknown-model pass-through with a safe default `get_model_info()` entry (full id against live cache → static catalog → default, never `None`).

- **`POLLINATIONS_*` env-var config (8 vars)** — `agentkthx/config.py`: `POLLINATIONS_BASE_URL` (`https://gen.pollinations.ai/v1`), `POLLINATIONS_API_KEY` (empty = anonymous tier), `POLLINATIONS_DEFAULT_MODEL` (`openai/gpt-5.4-nano`, card-verified 400K context), `POLLINATIONS_FALLBACK_MODEL` (`z-ai/glm-5.3-flash`), `POLLINATIONS_SAFE` (empty), `POLLINATIONS_FREE_ONLY` (false), `POLLINATIONS_ANON_CATALOG` (false), `POLLINATIONS_MAX_RETRIES` (5). Inline comments document the entitlement-scoping rationale on the two release-specific flags.

- **`scripts/probe_pollinations.sh` — GET-only, zero-pollen probe** — validates the reference doc against the live gateway: `/v1/models` fetch (anonymous or keyed via `POLLINATIONS_API_KEY`), category distribution, `gpt-5.4-nano` card spot-check, `healthy_fallbacks()` top-3 preview, zero-cost counts in BOTH encodings, and the v0.1.2 free-model discovery section against the bare `GET /models` feed: `paid_only` distribution, free-tier text counts, the TRUE zero-cost text list health-ordered, the healthiest free-tier community text top-10, and a keyed-vs-free-tier cross-check note (when a key was used, the keyed card count should equal the free-tier count — the entitlement-subset proof).

### Bug Fixes

- **`FREE_ONLY` with a key listed 0 models and burned pollen anyway** (user-reported from VM testing) — two stacked causes. (a) `_card_is_free()` could never match the gateway's currency-only zero-cost encoding (see discovery 2 above), so the `list_models()` filter kept nothing. (b) The generate-time FREE_ONLY redirect landed on `POLLINATIONS_FALLBACK_MODEL`, which is priced in every feed — the "fallback" burned the same pollen it was supposed to protect. **Fix (v0.1.1 + v0.1.2):** correct predicate + `_first_free_model()` redirect + `POLLINATIONS_ANON_CATALOG` browse toggle. Final VM matrix: 16 free models listed and generated against in every key state.

- **Keyed catalog browsing silently shrank 307 → 134 cards** (user-reported) — not a plugin bug but undocumented gateway entitlement scoping (see discovery 1 above). The keyed feed drops all 173 `paid_only` models, which also reshuffles `healthy_fallbacks()` rankings: the anonymous top-3 (all `paid_only=True`, two of them carrying `sr=100` off a single request) are invisible to a keyed feed, which instead ranks genuine entitlement models. **Fix:** `POLLINATIONS_ANON_CATALOG=1` restores the full public catalog for browsing while generation stays keyed.

### Tests

- **94 new tests in `tests/test_pollinations_backend.py` (1,236 lines, 18 classes):**
  - v0.1.0 (78 tests / 15 classes) — `TestManifestCompliance` (v0.2 form, alias in CLI flags, defaults), `TestCatalog` (static catalog integrity), `TestKeylessInit` (anonymous-tier contract, `sk_`/`pk_` validation), `TestURLsAndIdentity`, `TestNormalizeModelId` (three-layer alias resolution), `TestBodyConstruction` (wire-format deltas incl. `seed` passthrough, safe flags), `TestErrorEnvelope` (requestId surfaced), `TestErrorMapping` (402 non-retryable / 401 / 404 / 422 / 429 via mocked urlopen, Retry-After 60s cap), `TestParseResponse` (usage-null normalization), `TestHealthyFallbacks` (health ordering with fake cards), `TestListModels` (text filter, live cache), `TestModelDefaults` (context-length priority), `TestStreaming` (SSE contract), `TestPluginRegistration` (register/unregister lifecycle), `TestLiveAPI` (3 live-gated on `POLLINATIONS_API_KEY`).
  - v0.1.1 (+8) — `TestAnonCatalog` (3: flag strips the Authorization header from catalog GET / default keeps it / keyless never sends one), `TestFreeOnlyGenerate` (5: redirect prefers the healthiest free card / no-free degrades to the configured fallback with debug warning / an already-free model is left untouched / `_first_free_model()` health-ordered + text-only + empty-cache), plus a manifest-defaults assertion for the new key.
  - v0.1.2 (+8) — `TestZeroCostEncoding` (8: currency-only pricing is free / card-wrapper shape / zero-valued regression still free / priced, partial-zero, and empty pricing stay not-free / static-catalog regression / end-to-end FREE_ONLY filtering over a live-shaped catalog).

- Suite: 1660 → **1751 passed, 13 → 16 skipped, 0 failures** (+91 unit tests, +3 live-gated skips, 0 regressions).

### Documentation

- **`agentkthx/plugins/pollinations/plugin.json`** — v0.2 manifest; the description field carries the per-version change log (v0.1.1 browse toggle + redirect, v0.1.2 zero-cost encoding), and `config.defaults` documents all 8 env vars.
- **`agentkthx/plugins/pollinations/pollinations.py` module docstring** — full env-var surface, the two gateway-behavior discoveries (entitlement scoping with observed card counts; currency-only zero-cost encoding), and the free-tier vs zero-cost tier distinction.
- **`agentkthx/config.py`** — `POLLINATIONS_*` block with the entitlement-scoping rationale on `POLLINATIONS_ANON_CATALOG`.
- **`agentkthx/core/types.py`** — `BackendType.POLLINATIONS` enum entry pointing at the plugin directory and the technical reference doc.
- **Free-model truth table** (encoded in the probe script): bare `GET /models` carries the `paid_only` boundary; `/v1/models` cards carry pricing only (zero-cost = currency-only shape); a keyed `/v1/models` feed = the entitlement subset = the free-tier models only; health telemetry is a ROLLING window (request counts observed decreasing across runs), so `success_rate` comparisons across runs are only meaningful with the `requests` volume checked alongside.

### Known Limitations

- **Keyed + `ANON_CATALOG=1` fallback rankings may exceed the key's entitlement** — `healthy_fallbacks()` then ranks over the full public feed, where `paid_only` models dominate the top slots (8 of the anonymous top-10 at release time). Fine for browsing; a fallback pick may be a model the key cannot generate against (behavior against `paid_only` models with a free-tier key is untested).
- **Text/chat only** — image, video, audio, 3d, and embedding cards are visible in the catalog and listings but not exposed as generation tool surfaces (potential follow-up: media tool surfaces per the reference doc's tools chapter).
- **Live catalog churn** — community models are added and removed in real time (card count observed oscillating 307↔308 within one session, publisher paths renaming); run-to-run count differences of ±1–2 are expected gateway behavior, not plugin drift.
- **`model_family_config.py` family hint not added** (deliberate) — `detect_family` already substring-matches vendor names in provider/model ids; the doc's §13.2 suggestion was skipped as redundant.
- **Reference doc drift** — the doc's line-92 "bearer key returns the same data" claim is live-gateway-wrong (entitlement scoping), and its 311-card snapshot drifts daily; the live-card-first design absorbs this by construction.

### Audit Trail

- **Cumulative closure state: 37 of 98 findings (38%, mechanical table count — unchanged from R07.10; R07.11 is a feature add, not an audit closure release).** The audit.md prose-vs-table closure-count discrepancy noted in R07.10 (prose says 33) persists.
- **No new audit findings opened or closed in R07.11.** The 56 OPEN findings (including all 6 from R07.09) are unchanged.
- **The post-release audit pass for the Pollinations plugin code has not yet been run** (the R07.09 precedent: the audit pass runs after release and has historically opened findings — ROB-28/29-style catch-all exception handling and retry-loop duplication are the obvious first candidates here).
- **Plugin count: 11 → 12** (acp, bitnet, gemini, huggingface, mistral, openai, openrouter, orcarouter, pollinations, test-plugin, turboquant, zai). **Cloud backends: 6 → 7. Backend types: 10 → 11.**

## [R07.10] - 2026-09-27 6:19:08 PM

**web_search UA fix + rename, OpenRouter free-router whitelist, OpenRouter router null-max-tokens crash fix.** Four fixes bundled as a development release for GitHub: (1) the `web-search` tool was silently broken — DuckDuckGo now blocks non-browser User-Agents, so the prior `AgentKthx/1.0` UA returned "No results found" for every query (lite endpoint times out, html endpoint returns 400). The tool now spoofs a real Firefox UA + sends Accept/Accept-Language headers. Renamed `web-search` → `web_search` to match the Python function name and underscore convention used by every other builtin (`read_file`, `write_file`, `http_get`, `python_repl`). (2) `OPENROUTER_FREE_ONLY=true` filtered by `:free` suffix only — the named `openrouter/free` router (which auto-routes to the cheapest free model) was excluded. The fallback path also used a substring hack (`"free" in name` OR `flash/mini/haiku/tiny`) that incorrectly included paid flash variants. Both filter branches now use a proper `_is_free_model()` helper mirroring the OrcaRouter pattern. (3) `orcarouter/free` was already in OrcaRouter's whitelist — confirmed, no change needed. (4) **user-reported crash**: `agentkthx chat -m openrouter/free --backend openrouter` failed with `[Resilience] Fatal API error — not retrying: '<' not supported between instances of 'int' and 'NoneType'` — the OpenRouter API returns `max_completion_tokens: null` for the router (it's a router, not a real model, so no fixed output cap), and `dict.get("max_completion_tokens", 4096)` returns `None` (not the default 4096) when the key exists with value `None`. The `None` propagated through `_parse_openrouter_model` → `_get_model_defaults` → `_apply_max_tokens_cap` where `min(None, context_length // 32)` raised the TypeError. Fixed by coercing explicit `None` → 4096 at parse time. +6 new tests pin the OpenRouter free-router contract + the null-max-tokens crash regression. Suite 1654 → **1660 passed, 13 skipped, 0 failures**.

### Bug Fixes

- **`web_search` returned no results (UA blocked by DuckDuckGo)** — `agentkthx/tools/builtins.py` used `User-Agent: AgentKthx/1.0` for both `web_search()` and `http_get()`. DuckDuckGo now rejects non-browser UAs: `lite.duckduckgo.com/lite/` times out completely (endpoint appears retired), `html.duckduckgo.com/html/` returns HTTP 400. **Fix:** added module-level `_BROWSER_USER_AGENT` constant (defaults to `Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64; rv:156.0) Gecko/20100101 Firefox/156.0`, overridable via `AGENTKTHX_USER_AGENT` env var for future UA rotation). Applied to both `web_search()` and `http_get()`. Also added `Accept: text/html,application/xhtml+xml` and `Accept-Language: en-US,en;q=0.9` headers. Reordered `web_search()` endpoints: tries `html.duckduckgo.com` first (the endpoint that works in 2026, returns proper `<a class="result__a">` + `<a class="result__snippet">` structure with a browser UA), falls back to `lite.duckduckgo.com` (may be retired, but try once before giving up). The DDG redirect URL wrapper (`//duckduckgo.com/l/?uddg=URL`) is now unwrapped so callers get the actual destination URL.

- **`web-search` → `web_search` rename** — the prior hyphenated name was a leftover from when the tool was scoped as a skill before becoming a builtin. Every other builtin uses underscore convention (`read_file`, `write_file`, `http_get`, `python_repl`, `parse_json`, `count_words`, `count_chars`, `read_file_lines`, etc.). Renamed across 14 files: `agentkthx/tools/builtins.py` (registry name), `agentkthx/plugins/acp/acp_plugin.py` (4 sites: action map, tool manifest, id field, summary extractor), `agentkthx/core/error_recovery.py` (2 sites), `agentkthx/core/prompts.py` (2 sites), `agentkthx/core/tool_parse.py` (1 site), `agentkthx/skills/test-harness/SKILL.md`, `agentkthx/souls/nova-helper/AGENTS.md`, `agentkthx/souls/nova-trading/AGENTS.md` + `soul.json` + `SOUL.md` (4 sites), `tests/test_skills.py`, `docs/api/OPENROUTER_API_TECHNICAL_REFERENCE.md`, `docs/00_04_CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/ARCH.md`. Zero `web-search` strings remain. ACP external action type (`SEARCH`) unchanged — only the internal name was renamed.

- **`OPENROUTER_FREE_ONLY` excluded the named `openrouter/free` router** — `agentkthx/plugins/openrouter/openrouter.py` `list_models()` filtered by `model["name"].endswith(":free")` only. The named `openrouter/free` router (https://openrouter.ai/openrouter/free — auto-routes to the cheapest free model at request time) was filtered out, so users couldn't write `--model openrouter/free` with `FREE_ONLY` mode. The fallback path also used a substring hack (`"free" in name.lower()` OR `any(free in name.lower() for free in ["flash", "mini", "haiku", "tiny"])`) that incorrectly included paid flash variants (e.g. `glm-4.5-flash` is paid on ZAI). **Fix:** added `OPENROUTER_FREE_MODEL_WHITELIST: frozenset[str]` containing `openrouter/free` + a module-level `_is_free_model(model_id)` helper that returns `True` when the model ID is `openrouter/free` OR ends with `:free`. Both `list_models()` filter branches (live API path + catalog fallback) now use the helper. Mirrors the `ORCAROUTER_FREE_MODEL_WHITELIST` + `_is_free_model()` pattern in the OrcaRouter plugin — the two cloud backends now share the same whitelist enforcement shape.

- **`openrouter/free` crashed with `'<' not supported between instances of 'int' and 'NoneType'`** (user-reported) — `agentkthx/plugins/openrouter/openrouter.py` `_parse_openrouter_model` used `model_data.get("top_provider", {}).get("max_completion_tokens", 4096)`. The OpenRouter API returns `max_completion_tokens: null` explicitly for the `openrouter/free` router (it's a router, not a real model — no fixed output cap). `dict.get(k, default)` returns `None` (NOT the default 4096) when the key exists with value `None`. The `None` propagated through `_parse_openrouter_model` → `_get_model_defaults` → `OpenAICompatibleBackend._apply_max_tokens_cap` where `capped = min(max_tokens, context_length // 32)` raised `TypeError: '<' not supported between instances of 'int' and 'NoneType'` (Python's `min()` uses `<` internally). The agent loop's resilience layer caught the TypeError but classified it as terminal (not retryable), surfacing as `[Resilience] Fatal API error — not retrying: '<' not supported between instances of 'int' and 'NoneType'` with an empty response. **Fix:** `_parse_openrouter_model` now coerces explicit `None` → `4096` at parse time (`raw_max if raw_max is not None else 4096`). `_get_model_defaults` and `_get_model_info` switched from `details.get(k, default)` to `details.get(k) or default` so a `None` value is treated the same as an absent key. +2 regression tests pin the contract: parser coerces null → 4096, and `_get_model_defaults` returns int max_tokens (not None) for the router.

### Tests

- **6 new tests in `tests/test_openrouter_free_models.py`:**
  - `TestOpenRouterFreeRouter` (4 tests) — whitelist membership, `_is_free_model("openrouter/free") is True`, `:free`-suffix models are free, paid models (including `gpt-4o-mini` and `claude-3-haiku` that the prior substring hack would have misclassified) return False.
  - `TestOpenRouterRouterNullMaxTokens` (2 tests) — `_parse_openrouter_model` coerces `max_completion_tokens: null` → 4096 at parse time (the regression test for the user-reported crash); `_get_model_defaults('openrouter/free')` returns int `max_tokens` (not None), proving the `min(None, int)` crash in `_apply_max_tokens_cap` is fixed.

- Suite: 1654 → **1660 passed, 13 skipped, 0 failures** (+6 new tests, 0 regressions).

### Audit Trail

- **Cumulative closure state: 33 of 98 findings (34%)** across R07.00 → R07.08 (unchanged — R07.10 is a development release, not an audit closure).
- **No new audit findings opened or closed in R07.10.** The 6 R07.09 findings (MAINT-21, MAINT-22, ROB-28, ROB-29, SEC-18, TEST-09) remain OPEN. Their per-finding detail sections in `audit/audit.md` are still pending — the Findings Summary table is updated; the prose blocks aren't written yet.
- **Open count: 56** (unchanged from R07.09).
- **Plugin count: 11** (unchanged).
- **R07.09 release notes corrected in R07.09 audit-pass:** the original "no new audit findings opened by R07.09" claim was wrong; the post-release audit opened 6. Documented in the R07.09 Executive Summary.
